`delete_node(n6)`; snapshot and reopen; `set_prop(n5, tags, [x])`. A fresh build has `n0->n5` and
`n5->n0`; the live store's `neighbors` returns neither while `stats` reports two edges. With one
top-k rule, or with no snapshot, it does not happen. What disagrees after the restore — provenance
and topology, or the read overlay — was not established. The first engine item for 0.8.1.

**Closed, 0.8.1.** Two rules owned one edge. Every derived-edge insert in the engine asked the
post-snapshot overlay whether the edge was new (`Topology::add_edge`), and on a store opened from
a snapshot an edge a rule derived before it lives in the base. In `z1`, deleting `n6` sent `topk2`
looking for its next match for `n0`; it found `n0->n5`, which `topk1` had derived before the
snapshot, was told the edge was new, and recorded it as its own beside `topk1`: the rules counted
four edges for two. Changing `n5` then made `topk2` let go of the edge, the remove took it out of
the graph, and `topk1` went on counting it. Every derived-edge insert now goes through
`GraphMut::add_edge`, which counts the base's edges, so a second rule that desires an edge another
rule derived before the snapshot records nothing, as it always has without a snapshot.
`top_k_rules_edges_are_readable_after_a_snapshot_round_trip` failed first. The review's fuzz
(`rev58_fuzz.rs`, 60 seeds × 2 rule-name orders × 50 steps a mix, snapshots and reopens included):
ten of eleven mixes at 0 runs diverging from a fresh build at that count; `full-via` at 2 of 120,
the via-hop top-k half of row 83. The review's run at 100 seeds found `viatopk-plain` at 2 of 200 as
well, with no snapshot and the same on the commit before: nine of eleven. The cost is a binary search of the snapshot's row for the source on each
derived-edge insert into a store that has a snapshot. The first form of the fix merged the row
instead, which the review measured as quadratic in a source's degree: one write re-deriving a hub's
200,000 out-edges after a snapshot took 17.7 s, against 1.0 s before the fix and 1.03 s now.

The same fix closes a loss of a user's edge the ledger had not recorded, found by the review: a
user's `insert_edge`, a snapshot and a reopen, then a rule that desires the same edge — the rule
recorded the user's edge as its own, and deleted it when it stopped desiring it.

**Not repaired: a store snapshotted while two rules owned one edge.** A store whose double ownership
is still only in its WAL is repaired when it is opened. One that `main` snapshotted in that state is
not: the review found that neither a write, a snapshot nor `rebuild_rule` repairs it, a write can
still remove an edge a rule counts, and `stats.edges` stays wrong even after the rules are deleted
and created again. That repair is row 107.

## 104. Deleting a via node leaves the edge derived through it — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-14 by the same review (`x2_via_node_delete_leaves_edge`). Five operations and one
via-hop rule; `p->t` survives `delete_node(o)`. Row 50 is about the count `forget` reports when the
forgotten node is a via node, and may be this.

**Closed, 0.8.1.** The delete retracted only edges touching the deleted node, and the via node is
on neither end of the edge it carried. `on_node_removed_inner` now gathers, for every via-hop rule
the node is a via node of, the sources that hop through it — while the hop edges are still in the
graph — and evaluates them again with the node out of the hop. What that retracts goes through the
second pass, which a delete did not run before, so another rule over the same edge type that wants
the edge takes it; nothing is reclaimed for or onto the node being deleted.
`deleting_a_via_node_retracts_the_edges_derived_through_it`, for a plain and a top-k via rule,
failed first. The re-evaluation goes through the rule's index, as any other does; the first form
passed none, and deleting a via node that 50,000 sources hop through towards 10,000 destinations
took 146 s. Measured on the release build after the change: 42 ms for 10,000 sources and 10,000
destinations (29 ms on `main`, which leaves the edges), 374 ms for 50,000 sources and 100
destinations (133 ms). Row 50 is now visible: the delete removes those edges and its report still
says `derived_edges: 0`.

## 105. A self-label via-hop rule misses edges into a new node — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-14 by the same review (`p2_self_label_via_alone_insert_dst`). The review of that row's fix
pinned the cause: evaluating the new node as a source applies a desired set of its outgoing edges
against a retract set of every edge of the rule touching it, so an edge into it that another
source had just derived went again; inserted in the other order, the edge stayed.

**Closed, 0.8.1.** When a via-hop rule that links a label to itself re-evaluates a source, it now
retracts only that source's outgoing edges (`apply_desired_from`), on the ordinary change path, the
via-edge change path, the delete path and the second pass. The first form of the fix kept the
incoming edges by adding the set anchored on the source as a destination, which scans every source;
done once per affected source, a `set_prop` on a via node that 10,000 sources hop through took 93 s
where `main` took 60 ms. It now takes 27 ms. `a_self_label_via_rule_derives_the_edge_into_a_new_node`
failed first; the review's self-label fuzz mix went from 46 of 80 runs diverging to 0.

## 106. Deleting a top-k rule's best match re-picked it — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-14 by the second review of row 83's fix, with a fuzz built to break its guard.
Same on `main`, with no snapshot. `max_edges = None` was not affected.

**Closed, 0.8.1.** The node being deleted is dropped from the backfill's candidates, and from the
second pass when a delete runs it. `deleting_a_top_k_rules_best_match_promotes_the_next_one`, for
`k = 1` and `k = 2`, failed first.

Rows 104 to 106 together, measured with the review's fuzz (`rev58_fuzz.rs`, 40 seeds × 2 rule-name
orders × 50 steps a mix): nine of its eleven mixes went to 0 runs diverging from a fresh build. The
two left, 6 and 2 of 80, are row 103 (a snapshot and a reopen in each) and the via-hop top-k half
of row 83.

## 107. Stores damaged by row 103 are not repaired — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-15 by the review of row 103's fix, which wrote stores on `main` with `z1`'s prefix
and opened them on the fixed build. A store with the double ownership still in its WAL replays
clean. A store snapshotted in that state does not: the owners and the count survive a write, a
snapshot and `rebuild_rule`, and deleting and creating both rules repairs the owners but leaves
`stats.edges` counting edges that are not there, across reopens. The review's suggestions, cheapest
first: when a rule retracts an edge another rule's provenance still holds, keep the edge; or dedupe
provenance at open, keeping the first owner in name order; and recount `edge_count` from the merged
rows the snapshot encoder already builds, which also corrects the undercount a tombstone over an
absent edge leaves.

**Closed, 0.8.2.** Two changes repair a damaged store at the first write after opening it:

1. `ensure_provenance_loaded_mut` (`crates/core-rules/src/engine.rs`) deduplicates the decoded
   provenance before the live fields are populated: for each `(etype, src, dst)` triple, only the
   first rule in BTree (byte order on rule name) order keeps ownership, a deterministic choice;
   later evaluations settle ownership and the pass is idempotent on a clean store.  The pass is
   O(n log n) in provenance entries.  Until the first write, `stats().rules`, `explain`, and
   `open_at` still report both owners.  The deduped edge keeps the dropped rule's weight property
   until the edge is re-derived; a write to its source alone does not correct it.

2. `topology_merge_to_csr` (`crates/core-storage/src/v8/encode.rs`) recounts `edge_count` by
   summing the out-row lengths of the merged CSR directly, instead of carrying the header's stored
   count forward arithmetically.  This makes every new snapshot's header correct regardless of the
   old header's value.

`GraphDb` (`crates/core-api/src/db.rs`) adds `base_edge_count_from_rows`, computed at open and
refreshed after each snapshot by walking the base CSR's out-rows, and uses it in `edge_count()` and
`stats().edges` in place of the snapshot header's `edge_count` field.  A test hook
`inject_double_ownership_for_test` (gated `test-hooks` feature) reproduces the damaged state; run
with `cargo test -p mushroomdb --features test-hooks --test shared_edge_rules`.

Stores where the edge was already lost before 0.8.2 (the losing write ran while the store was open
on 0.8.0 or 0.8.1) are not repaired: provenance still names an owner for an edge the graph no
longer holds, and the dedup pass, which removes second owners only, has nothing to correct.  Row
111 repairs those stores, in 0.9.1.

## 108. A user edge over a rule's, after a snapshot, is not refused — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-15 by the review of row 103's fix. The ownership check `insert_edge` makes reads
what is written since the snapshot, so a rule's edge that lives in the snapshot is reported as
present rather than as the rule's. Nothing is lost; the caller is told less than without a snapshot.

**Closed, 0.9.1.** Only the first write after a clean open was affected: the handle that took the
snapshot answered `RuleOwned`. A clean open leaves the snapshot's sections unread, and the check
ran before the commit read them, so it saw no rule owning anything. `insert_edge`, `delete_edge`,
`delete_node` and every batch now read the snapshot's provenance before their checks
(`load_provenance_for_write` in `crates/core-api/src/db.rs`). `delete_edge` was already refused, by
the check that a live rule would derive the edge again. Tests in
`crates/core-api/tests/first_write_after_open.rs`; the single insert and the batch insert failed
first.

## 109. A tombstone over an absent base edge under-counts the live edge count — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-15 by the review of row 107's fix. A rule that retracts an edge the snapshot's
base does not hold — one that lived only in the overlay, or was never there — still records a
tombstone (`Topology::remove_edge` tombstones whatever it does not find in the overlay). Row 107's
encoder fix counts a new snapshot's edges from its rows, so every snapshot's header is right; but
the live count, `corrected_edge_count`, is base rows minus tombstones plus the overlay, and reads
one low for each such tombstone until the next snapshot. Probe: `stats.edges` = 0 with one edge
present. The review's cross-version fuzz ended one low in 2 of 60 `full-topk` runs.

Fix: record the tombstone only when the base holds the edge (`base_has_out_edge`).

**Closed, 0.9.1.** Every derived-edge removal in the engine goes through `GraphMut::remove_edge`,
which tombstones only an edge the snapshot holds and otherwise removes it from the overlay alone
(`Topology::remove_overlay_edge`). `retracting_an_edge_the_graph_does_not_hold_leaves_the_edge_count_right`,
on a store the real 0.8.0 release wrote (`tests/fixtures/v080_lost_edge`), failed first with
`stats.edges` = 0 and one edge present. Row 111's repair now runs before any such retraction on
that store, so the test passes with either fix alone. The review found no other way to reach a
retraction of an edge neither the snapshot nor the overlay holds, so the routing is defence in
depth, and no test fails without it; the unit test
`remove_overlay_edge_records_no_tombstone_for_an_edge_the_overlay_lacks` covers only the topology
half.

## 110. A first-write `delete_node` on a snapshotted store misreports its counts — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-15 by the review of row 107's fix. On a store opened from a snapshot, a
`delete_node` that is the first write reports the deleted node's rule-derived edges as manual:
`DeleteReport { manual_edges: 2, derived_edges: 0 }` for two rule edges, on 0.8.1 as well. The
report is computed from provenance before the commit has loaded it; the commit itself goes through
`log_then_apply_with`, which loads the snapshot's sections and provenance first, so the edges are
removed correctly and row 107's dedup runs. Only the report is wrong. Row 50 is about the same
report's derived count for a via node, and may share this.

**Closed, 0.9.1.** The report now reads the snapshot's provenance first, as the commit does (row
108's change). `deleting_a_node_first_after_open_counts_its_derived_edges_as_derived` failed first
with the report above.

## 111. Stores where the edge was already lost before 0.8.2 are not repaired — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-15 by the review of row 107's fix.  Row 107's repair works by deduplicating
provenance at the first write: if two rules both claim a triple, the second (by byte order on rule
name) loses its claim, so when it retracts the triple the first rule still owns it and the edge
survives.  But if the losing write already ran on 0.8.0 or 0.8.1 — before 0.8.2 — the graph no
longer holds the edge while provenance still names a rule as its owner: the rule counts an edge
that is not there (`counted=2 present=0`). The dedup pass removes second owners only, so it has
nothing to correct.

Suggested fix: at the first write after open, find every provenance triple
whose `(etype, src, dst)` is absent from the current topology and re-evaluate those source nodes
against all rules.  Each surviving triple then has exactly one owner and the graph reflects what
the rules want.  This is O(provenance) in the scan and at most O(affected sources × rules) in
re-evaluation.

**Closed, 0.9.1.** `RuleEngine::reconcile_lost_provenance` (`crates/core-rules/src/engine.rs`) runs
once per open of a store with a snapshot, before the first commit that is not a declaration: it
drops each provenance triple the graph does not hold, recording no retraction, and re-evaluates its
source against every rule over that edge type, the owner included. WAL replay runs it at the same
point, so a reopen gives the same graph. Until that first write, reads show the store as it was
opened. `the_first_write_after_open_restores_the_edges_080_had_lost` opens a store the real 0.8.0
release wrote, makes an unrelated write, and compares the result with a store built fresh from the
same nodes and rules, before and after a WAL reopen and through `open_at`; it failed first with no
edges present. An as-of handle replays without `apply_frames_over`, and runs the repair from its own
replay loops; the review of the fix found it missing there (36 `open_at` divergences on three
lost-edge stores), and the `open_at` assertion failed without it. History shows the edges returning
at that first write: `DerivedEdgeAdded` markers and subscriber events, as for any derived edge.

`delete_node`'s report, computed before the commit, now counts only the derived edges the graph
holds, so on such a store a node's hand-written edge is no longer reported as derived
(`deleting_a_node_first_on_a_store_that_lost_edges_counts_its_hand_written_edge` failed first, with
`(0, 2)`). `forget` of a property, as that first write, still reports the edges it compares before
the repair: row 115.

Measured by the review, release build, about 1M derived edges and nothing lost: the first write
after open 1.188 s before and 1.208 s after (+20 ms, 1.7 %); later writes unchanged within noise.
The cross-version fuzz (420 stores the real 0.8.0 release wrote) went from 19 of 26 damaged stores
still differing from a fresh build after the first write to 6; those 6 are row 113.

## 112. `open_at` skips row 107's repair — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-16 by the review of row 111's fix, with `DeleteRule` on the 0.8.0 stores
`nat080`, `v080/snapshotted` and `w080`: 14 as-of reads differ from the live handle, before and
after that fix.

**Closed, 0.9.2.** `from_persist` (`crates/core-rules/src/engine.rs`) now calls `dedup_provenance` on the provenance map before building `owned`, the same dedup `ensure_provenance_loaded_mut` runs for a regular open. `open_at_deduplicates_double_owned_provenance` (`crates/core-api/tests/shared_edge_rules.rs`) opens the `v080_snapshotted` fixture at a commit above the floor (added by a dummy insert so the WAL is non-empty) and verifies that `explain` shows one owner for the previously double-owned edge.

## 113. A lost top-k edge no rule counts is not re-derived — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-16 by the review of row 111's fix. Example: `two-topk`, reversed rule names, seed
10, first write `AddLink(n4, n7)`: `SAME n7→n2` stays missing and `b_topk1` owns no edge. The
namespaced corpus has the same case (`full-topk-true-1`, `SAME n6→n0`).

**Closed, 0.9.2.** `reconcile_lost_provenance` (`crates/core-api/src/db.rs`) no longer returns early when no provenance-named edges are missing from the graph: it always calls the new `fill_undertopk_sources` pass, which finds top-k rule sources whose provenance count is below cap and re-evaluates them. `undertopk_pass_restores_edge_absent_from_both_graph_and_provenance` (`crates/core-api/tests/shared_edge_rules.rs`, `test-hooks` feature) injects double ownership to produce the row-113 damage, then verifies the first write restores the edge.

## 114. A reader's `node_edges` never says an edge is derived — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-16 by the review of row 111's fix. `ReaderSnapshot::node_edges` sets `derived`
to `false` for every edge.

**Closed, 0.10.** The reader holds no rule engine, so the writer hands it the answer, in three
parts that a read lays over one another, newest first.

- *The base.* Every snapshot with a rule-derived edge carries a new optional section 17
  (`DERIVED_BITS`, `docs/format-stability.md`): one bit per `u32` of the TOPOLOGY section, set for
  the neighbour entries of derived edges in both the out-row and the in-row. A reader that has
  found a node's base row (the lookup `node_edges` already makes) reads the bit at the edge's own
  entry: no search, no table, nothing built at open or at the first read. A snapshot without the
  section (written by 0.9.x, or with no derived edge) is answered from a node-indexed table built
  from PROVENANCE by the first read that needs it (about 50 ms on a store with 900k derived
  edges); a store with no snapshot, and an as-of view, take the engine's provenance whole when
  state is loaded. 0.9.x ignores the section and a 0.9.x snapshot leaves it out.
- *The folds.* The engine journals each triple's final state
  (`RuleEngine::take_derived_changes`) and every fold lays that journal over the previous one as
  a layer, so a write-path fold, a refresh and a change of roles cost what changed, not the size of
  provenance. Layers are compacted past eight, keeping only the difference from the base, and a
  snapshot starts over on the section it just wrote.
- *The unfolded tail.* One index (`TailDerived`) holds the derived changes of every commit since
  the last fold, grouped by node and appended to as each commit is recorded, so a read asks the
  tail one question.

`node_edges` and `node_edges_scoped` then report the flag the live handle's `node_edges` does,
for every case in `crates/core-api/tests/reader_derived.rs`: no snapshot, after a fold, after a
snapshot, after a reopen, a snapshot with a WAL tail then reopen, edges derived in the tail over a
base, a retraction, a dropped rule, enough folds to compact the layers, batches that retract and
re-derive, a role mask, a snapshot with section 17 and one with it stripped, each reopened, over
a tail, after another snapshot and under `open_at`. The mapping from provenance to bits is pinned
in `crates/core-storage/src/v8/seam.rs` (`derived_bits_mark_exactly_the_owned_edges`). One path
has no test of its own: a role-definition change refolds from the same journal and tail as any
other fold, but `commit_roles` is crate-private. Its ignored `reader_derived_bench` is the timing
probe.

## 117. The lock-free reader can miss a derived edge a batch retracts and re-derives — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-20 by the review of row 114's fix. A commit's derived-edge changes reach the
reader (`CommitDelta`) as a list of inserts and a list of retractions, and the order between them
was lost. A batch such as `set c.team = blue; set c.team = red` retracts and then re-derives the
edge `a → c`; the reader replayed the inserts and then the retractions, so in the unfolded tail
(fewer than 64 commits since the fold) `reader().node_edges` and every query through the reader
(the HTTP read routes) dropped that edge although the store has it. 4 edges on the live handle, 2
seen by the reader in the probe that found it. Present in released 0.9.x; the live handle and the
snapshot on disk were never wrong, and a reload (reopen, refresh) restored the reader.

**Closed, 0.10.** `log_then_apply_with` (`crates/core-api/src/db.rs`) nets each triple to its final
state in emission order when it builds the commit's delta, so the two lists never name the same
triple and the order cannot matter. `a_batch_that_retracts_then_re_derives`,
`a_batch_that_re_derives_then_retracts` and `a_batch_that_does_both_several_times`
(`crates/core-api/tests/reader_derived.rs`) compare the reader with the live handle in the tail,
after the next fold, after a snapshot and after a reopen; they fail on the previous netting.
## 115. `forget` of a property misreports on a store row 111 repairs — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-16 by the review of row 111's fix. Fix: run row 111's repair before the report is
computed, staging its deltas so the next commit carries them.

**Closed, 0.9.2.** `ensure_reconcile_for_report` (`crates/core-api/src/db.rs`) loads provenance, runs `reconcile_lost_provenance`, and drains the engine's pending deltas; it is called from `forget` (`crates/core-api/src/memory/forget.rs`) for `ForgetTarget::Prop` before the before-snapshot is taken. `forgetting_a_prop_on_damaged_store_reports_derived_edges` (`crates/core-api/tests/shared_edge_rules.rs`) opens the `v080_lost_edge` fixture and verifies `report.derived_edges >= 1`.

## 116. Row 113's repair cost every open — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-19, after #72 merged and before the 0.9.2 tag, by timing open, first write and
second write against v0.9.1 (median of five, release build, macOS). The independent review of
#72 had reasoned that the top-k pass "finds nothing to do" on a healthy store; that holds only
for sources at their cap. A source with fewer candidates than its cap is short of k on any store,
so the pass built every index and re-evaluated those sources at the first commit of every open —
in WAL replay, where an open pays it. All rules in the memory schema are top-k (caps 32 and 512)
and real entities sit far below them.

| Store (top-k, under cap) | open, 0.9.1 | open, `main` @ d953744 |
|---|---|---|
| 6k nodes | 6.0 ms | 8.5 ms |
| 60k nodes | 66.5 ms | 90.3 ms |
| 150k nodes | 172.4 ms | 247.4 ms |

The first and second writes did not move (4–5 ms), and stores at their cap or with no top-k rule
did not either.

**Closed, 0.9.2.** A snapshot now says when the state it holds has had the repair: section 16,
`PROV_RECONCILED`, four bytes `MREC` (`crates/core-storage/src/v8/mod.rs`,
`crates/core-storage/src/v8/encode.rs`). The writer sets it from
`RuleEngine::lost_provenance_reconciled`, true when the handle ran the repair, opened a snapshot
that says so, or opened none (`crates/core-api/src/db.rs`); a test hook that injects damage
withholds it. An open of a marked snapshot skips the repair, which on such a state changes nothing,
so live, replay and as-of reads agree by construction. A store whose snapshot predates the mark
pays the repair at each open until its next snapshot.

Measured after the fix against `main` @ d953744 on one machine (release build, median of seven,
two interleaved runs each; a top-k rule with every source under its cap, plus a plain rule):

| Store | open, d953744 | open, fixed | open, same store with no top-k rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| ~60k nodes | 89.0–89.9 ms | 62.5–62.9 ms | 62.9–64.8 ms |
| ~150k nodes | 237.4–241.3 ms | 162.7–171.7 ms | 164.4–168.9 ms |

First and second writes stayed at 4–6 ms in both builds. Tests in
`crates/core-api/tests/shared_edge_rules.rs`: `a_snapshot_taken_before_the_repair_does_not_say_it_ran`,
`a_snapshot_taken_after_the_repair_says_so_and_reads_the_same`, `a_first_snapshot_says_the_repair_ran`,
`an_open_of_a_marked_snapshot_skips_the_repair` and `injected_damage_withholds_the_mark` (the last
two under `test-hooks`).

## 118. Batch validation is quadratic in the batch size — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-20 by the first throughput run of `mushroomdb import` (PF-03a), which had been
expected to reach 100k nodes/s and ran at 1.8k. A profile put 6,727 of 6,754 samples in
`MutPreview::note_insert_node` (`crates/core-api/src/db.rs`). A batch is validated against an
overlay of the ops already accepted in it, and inserting a node removed that key's earlier overlay
properties with `BTreeMap::retain`, which visits every entry: N inserts of a few properties each
cost O(N²) key comparisons. The plain `ingest` path (`GraphDb::ingest`, one `write_batch`) had the
same cost and is present in released 0.9.2; nothing but the batch size changed it.

| Rows, 50k batches | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| 100k nodes, `GraphDb::ingest` | 41.0–41.5 s (2.4k n/s) | 0.85–0.96 s (104–118k n/s) |
| 200k nodes, `mushroomdb import`, `--batch-size 10000` | 12.9 s | not re-measured at 10,000; 2.6 s at 50,000 |

**Closed, 0.10.** `Overlay::take_props_of` and `Overlay::take_removed_props_of` find a key's
entries with a range lookup on the ordered `(key, field)` pairs, O(log N) plus the entries that
key has. `note_insert_node`, `note_delete_node` and the rename migration (`note_rename_node`) use
them; what is removed and moved is the same set. One part is not changed: `note_delete_node` and
`note_rename_node` still scan the batch's overlay *edges* (keyed by type, source, destination), so
a batch that both inserts very many edges and deletes or renames very many nodes keeps a cost
proportional to the two. Tests in `crates/core-api/tests/batch_vs_commits.rs`:
`random_batches_with_key_reuse_agree_with_separate_commits` (300 randomized batches over five keys
with insert, set, remove, delete, rename and Replace/Skip upserts of one key beside a plain and a
via-hop rule, compared with the same ops as separate commits: live, after a reopen, and read back
at the last commit through `open_at`; at least 100 must be accepted so the overlay is reached) and
`insert_set_delete_insert_of_one_key_agrees`. The independent review of the change also compared
2,500 randomized batches across 1,773 reopened, snapshotted and `open_at` states against base,
byte-identical.

## 119. A snapshot of a store with no commits fails — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-20 by the review of PF-03a: a header-only CSV imported nothing and then failed
with "No such file or directory", and `mushroomdb snapshot <fresh-dir>` failed the same way. A
store that has never committed has no `wal.bin`. `snapshot_with` wrote `snapshot.bin` and
`snapshot.intent`, then, with `archive_wal` set, took the "archive the WAL" branch because no
archive existed yet (the branch that skips an empty WAL requires one) and renamed the missing
file. The intent stayed behind.

**Closed, 0.10.** A WAL with no bytes has nothing to archive whether or not an archive exists;
the replacement WAL is written and nothing is renamed. `mushroomdb import` also skips the snapshot
when it committed nothing. Tests in `crates/core-api/tests/snapshot_empty_store.rs`
(`a_snapshot_of_a_store_with_no_commits_succeeds_and_leaves_no_intent` in all three WAL modes,
`a_snapshot_of_a_store_with_only_a_declaration_keeps_the_declaration`,
`the_first_archive_after_an_empty_snapshot_still_holds_the_history`) and
`csv_import_header_only_files_import_nothing_and_leave_no_intent` in `crates/cli/tests/csv_import.rs`.

## 120. CSV import refuses relationship property columns — DEFERRED

`mushroomdb import` (PF-03a) takes `:START_ID`, `:END_ID` and `:TYPE` from a relationships file
and nothing else: the engine's edges carry no user properties, so a property column has nowhere to
go. The import refuses such a header with the column named, at file line 1, instead of loading the
edges and dropping the values. Supporting the columns needs edge properties in the engine's write
path and WAL, which is a format change.

**DEFERRED to 0.11.**

**Update (0.10, QL-RP).** The engine half is done: edges carry user properties
in the write path and the WAL. Cypher writes them (`CREATE (a)-[:T {k: v}]->(b)`,
`MERGE` with a relationship map, `SET r.p`, `SET r += …`, `SET r = …`,
`REMOVE r.p`) as `BatchOp::SetEdgeProp` / `BatchOp::RemoveEdgeProp`, logged as the
V11 records `SetEdgePropId` (28) and `RemoveEdgePropId` (29) and stored where a
rule's weights already were (the edge-property overlay, snapshot section 5). What
remains for 0.11 is the import mapping property columns onto those ops, and the
JSONL export carrying them.

## 121. A reload can mix two snapshots' state under the newer identities — DEFERRED to 0.10

Found on 2026-10-20 by the second re-review of the 0.10 backup worker (HO-07). Present in
released 0.9.x.

`load_from_disk` reads `snapshot.bin` first and replays `wal.bin` after it, but it records
`snapshot_ident` only after the replay. Suppose another process completes both renames of a
WAL-replacing snapshot (new `snapshot.bin`, then the baseline `wal.bin`) between those two points.
The handle then holds the old snapshot's state plus the new baseline WAL, recorded under the new
snapshot's identity. No later staleness check or refresh sees a difference, so the handle stays
mixed until the next snapshot changes the identity again.

The WAL-file identity that HO-07 added to `refresh` does not close this, because that identity is
also taken after the rename. The fix is to take the snapshot's identity from the file actually
read, before reading it, and to re-check it after the replay, reloading when it moved. That is an
engine follow-up, outside the backup unit that found it.

## 122. On non-Unix platforms an equal-length WAL replacement followed by a write leaves a store that does not open — DEFERRED, target not yet set

Found on 2026-10-20 by the fourth re-review of the 0.10 backup worker (HO-07). Present in
released 0.9.x. Affects Windows and other non-Unix platforms only. Windows x86_64 is a supported
binary.

Before every append on a cross-process write scope, `enter_write_scope` calls
`refresh_checking(true)`. That compares the WAL file's identity with the one this handle's cursor
describes. On Unix the identity is the inode. Elsewhere `file_ident` returns `None`, so the
comparison is `None` against `None` and never fires. Suppose another process, or anything outside
mushroomdb, replaces `wal.bin` with a different file of exactly this handle's cursor length while
the snapshot stays the same. Then neither the length check nor the snapshot identity can see the
change. The handle's next write appends frames encoded against its own interner and id state onto
the new file, and a fresh open then fails with `Corrupt` (for example "wal intern assigned 1 for
"A", record wanted 0").

**Severity: Medium.** The trigger is narrow. It takes an external WAL replacement, or the
row-121 reload race in a peer's snapshot window, followed by peer writes that stop exactly at the
old cursor length. But the consequence is a store that cannot be opened, not a stale read. Frames
in agent-memory stores are often the same size, so an exact length match is plausible.

**Mitigation, documented in `docs/site/multiprocess.md`:** on non-Unix, do not replace a store's
WAL from outside while other processes have it open.

**Fix:** a platform-specific identity check. On Windows, take the volume serial number and file
index from `GetFileInformationByHandle` (`nFileIndexHigh`/`nFileIndexLow`) and return them from
`file_ident`. The existing comparison then covers the case unchanged. The Unix test
`a_write_after_an_equal_length_wal_replacement_leaves_an_openable_store`
(`crates/core-api/tests/durable_wal.rs`) should then run on Windows as well. Owner: engine
follow-up.
## 123. An equality index over a view property goes stale — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-20 while implementing 0.10 TY-8. A view writes its property inside `apply`
(`crates/core-rules/src/views.rs`), which does not pass the property index, so the postings for
`(view label, view_prop)` keep the value the node had when the index last saw it. Probe: a Degree
view `deg` on `User`, `enable_index("User", "deg")`, two users and one `KNOWS` edge; `MATCH (n:User)
WHERE n.deg = 1` returns 0 rows live and 1 after a reopen, when `rebuild_all` reads the columns. A
reopened store and a never-closed one disagree. TY-5 refuses a unique constraint over a view
property and TY-8 refuses a range index over one, both ways; `enable_index` still accepted one, and
a store that already had such an index kept it.

**Closed, 0.10 (TY-5b).** Refused both ways, as TY-5 and TY-8 do: `enable_index` fails with
`property '<f>' on label '<L>' is owned by view '<v>'; an equality index cannot cover it`, and
`create_view` fails with `property '<f>' on label '<L>' is covered by an equality index; a view
cannot own it`, each before the WAL write. Maintaining the index from the view write path was the
larger change: views write inside `apply` on every edge, property and node path, and each of those
would have to carry the index. A store written before the refusal that holds both still opens:
after replay `drop_indexes_over_view_properties` (`crates/core-api/src/db.rs`) forgets the equality
declaration for that pair, so a query on it scans and answers as it does after a reopen, and the
next snapshot no longer re-emits the declaration. Tests: `an_equality_index_cannot_cover_a_view_property`
and `a_store_with_an_index_over_a_view_property_opens_and_answers_correctly`
(`crates/core-api/tests/range_index.rs`, default build).

## 124. The reader reads a weighted rule's recent scores as null — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-20 by TX-5, when moving full-token `POST /query` onto the lock-free reader made
`crates/server/tests/e2e.rs::end_to_end_demo_http_watch_mcp` read `r.score` as `null`. The defect
is older: in 0.9.x a role-bound token's `/query` already ran on `ReaderSnapshot`, as does any
caller of the public `SharedDb::reader()`.

A rule with a `weight_prop` writes each derived edge's score into `EdgeProps` itself, and refreshes
it in place when an endpoint changes, with no WAL record of its own. `CommitDelta` carried the
commit's records, its derived inserts and retractions and its view values, but no edge
properties, so a snapshot's effective state held the score only once a fold had copied it. Every
score written since the last fold read as `null`.

**Closed, 0.10 (TX-5).** `EdgeProps` journals every key `set` or `remove_edge` writes
(in memory, `#[serde(skip)]`; the format is unchanged). The writer drains the journal once per
commit into `CommitDelta.edge_props` as exact overlay entries (properties and tombstone), the
reader restores them after the commit's records and derived edges, and a fold clears the journal.
Tests: `writes_are_journalled_and_an_entry_restores_exactly` in
`crates/core-storage/src/edge_props.rs`; the `FITS … r.score` cases of `read_parity_tests` in
`crates/server/src/http.rs` (full-token and role-token paths, against `GraphDb::query_masked`);
the weighted-rule steps of `every_snapshot_matches_the_live_handle_and_keeps_its_own_answer` in
`crates/core-api/tests/reader_shared_effective.rs`; and
`each_kind_of_write_is_read_by_the_next_request` in `crates/server/tests/concurrent_reads.rs`.
Disabling the restore fails the parity case in three store states and the chain test.

## 125. The equality index treated `Int` 2 and `Float` 2.0 as different — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-20 by the review of 0.10 TY-8. `PropertyIndex::lookup` probed the one
`ValueKey` of the operand, and keys are by variant, so `MATCH (n:L {v: 2})` on an indexed pair
found the `Int` 2 only, where the scan's `values_equal` — numeric across `Int` and `Float` — also
finds the `Float` 2.0; `0` likewise missed `-0.0`. Declaring an index changed the answer. The
defect predates 0.10; TY-8 made it reachable through range indexes, which also answer equality.

**Closed, 0.10 (TY-8).** `UnifiedIndex::lookup` (`crates/core-storage/src/range_index.rs`) answers
as `values_equal` does: an `Int` also probes its `Float` value, a `Float` probes the `Int`s whose
conversion equals it (one below 2^53, a run of neighbours above), the two zeros find each other, and
`NaN` finds nothing. An operand without an index key (list, map, duration) is answered by a scan
(`GraphView::nodes_with_prop`, `crates/core-query/src/view.rs`). Tests: `an_equality_index_finds_int_and_float_alike`
(`crates/core-api/tests/range_index.rs`, default build), `equality_is_numeric_as_the_scan_is`
(unit), and the randomized `the_index_answers_every_comparison_exactly_as_the_scan`
(`crates/core-query/tests/index_matches_scan.rs`), which compares the index with `eval_cmp` over a
scan.

## 126. The first write after a fold copies store structures under the write lock — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-21 by the review of TX-1b (the incremental reader and transaction overlay). After a
fold, the write path's copy-on-write clones structures the new base shares, among them the property
index and the column store, so the first commit after each fold takes time proportional to the store
while it holds the write lock. It happens once per fold, not once per commit, and the same fold and
copy exist in released 0.9.2; TX-1b made it smaller by no longer sharing the full-text index. TX-5's
fix round 3 measures it at 100k and 1M nodes.

**DEFERRED to TX-7 (0.10).** TX-7 owns the read and write latency gates; the fix is structural sharing
or a patch of the structures the commit touches, so the copy no longer scales with the store.

**Closed, 0.10 (TX-7).** The shared structures are now copy-on-write in pieces (new module
`crates/core-storage/src/cow.rs`), so a clone shares them and a write copies only the pieces it lands in:

- `ColumnStore`: each column behind its own `Arc`; every typed column, presence bitmap and adapter a
  `CowVec` (chunks of 128); a mixed column a `CowMap`; the string intern keeps its strings once, in a
  `CowVec`, and finds them through a `HashIndex` of hashes in 1,024 shards; prop tombstones a `CowMap`.
- `UnifiedIndex` (the equality and range index): postings, reverse map and offsets are `CowMap`s, a
  persistent B+tree of `Arc`ed nodes of at most 32 entries; a posting is an `IdSet`, inline for one id.
- `IdMap`: keys in a `CowVec`, found through a `HashIndex`; tombstones a `CowMap`. Its serialized form is
  unchanged.

- The interner, topology overlay and edge properties: the write path handed the rule engine `&mut`
  to them through `Arc::make_mut`, so every commit that ran the engine (every property write) copied all
  three after a fold, though most derive nothing. `GraphMut` now holds them as `CowMut`, which copies
  a shared `Arc` only on the engine's first write (fix round 1). Pinned by
  `db::tests::a_write_that_derives_nothing_copies_no_topology`.

The fold still takes `Arc` clones; the first commit after it copies a path of nodes or a chunk per
structure it writes. TX-5's published snapshots and TX-1b's layer read the fold's clones, which no write
reaches; `crates/core-api/tests/fold_copy_on_write.rs` pins that (reader snapshots held across 900
writes and ~14 folds keep their answers; live, reopened, `open_at`, snapshotting and not all agree).

Measured with `crates/server/examples/fold_costs.rs` (`CLOCK=cpu PASSES=2 BULKS=40`: writer-thread CPU
time, fsync off, `memory_defaults()` store, `name` full-text and equality indexed; the 40 bulk batches
grow the stores to 500k and 1.4M nodes), median of 5 interleaved runs, load 5.3–14.8 (provisional):

| First commit after a fold | v0.9.2 | TX-7 base | TX-7 |
|---|---|---|---|
| `name`, 500k nodes | 164.5 ms | 151.1 ms | 0.46 ms |
| `name`, 1.4M nodes | 426.9 ms | 465.0 ms | 0.42 ms |
| `score` (plain), 500k nodes | 33.3 ms | 41.2 ms | 0.27 ms |
| `score` (plain), 1.4M nodes | 104.2 ms | 140.2 ms | 0.31 ms |
| `score` (plain), 1M nodes and 2M edges, no snapshot (fix round 1, load 6.2–14.7) | 176.9 ms | 236.6 ms | 0.39 ms |

The 10k-row bulk commit's worst case falls from 738 ms to 104 ms at 1M nodes; what remains was not
profiled, and is expected to be the pieces such a batch copies the first time it touches them after a
fold (the dense label vector is still copied whole, and a write that derives or inserts an edge
copies the topology overlay whole: row 137). Its median rises from 51 ms to
77 ms against the TX-7 base (0.42× v0.9.2), and its mean from 71 to 77 ms: the trees cost more per insert
than the flat maps did. See the TX-7 report.


## 127. `serve` on an unwritable data directory reported `store is busy: write lock held` — CLOSED by HO-12b

Found on 2026-10-21 while writing the volume-ownership recipe for the deploy templates (HO-12).
Present in released 0.9.x. The image runs as uid 65532 and Fly and Render mount a new volume
root-owned, so the server could not create `LOCK` in `/data`.

`SharedDb::write()` called `poll_cross_process_lock(..).unwrap_or(false)`. The permission error from
opening `LOCK` became "lock not acquired", the first mutation answered `GraphError::Busy`, and
`serve` printed `store is busy: write lock held by another process`. The group-commit drain thread
treated the same error as a degraded handle. Nothing held a lock.

**Severity: Medium.** No data is at risk, but the message names the wrong cause and the fix is
unguessable from it.

**Fix (HO-12b):** `GraphError::Unwritable { path, detail, read_only_filesystem }` names the path,
the effective uid, the directory's owner and mode, and what to change; `Busy` is kept for a lock
another process holds. Tests: `crates/core-api/tests/unwritable_dir.rs`,
`crates/cli/tests/serve_unwritable.rs`.

## 128. `SET n.id` changes the `id` property without renaming the node key — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-21 in the QL-06 review. Present in released 0.9.x.

`CREATE (n:L {id: 'alice'})` stores `id` as an ordinary property equal to the node's key. A
Cypher `MATCH … SET n.id = 'mallory'` was a plain `SetProp` with no special case, so the property
changed and the key did not: the node stayed reachable as `alice` while `id` said `mallory`, and
`REMOVE n.id` deleted the property outright. The same held for `set_prop`, a batch, CAS, an
upsert, ingest and the HTTP and Python property calls. `rename_node` moves a stored `id` with the
key only while the two are equal, so after a divergence it stopped doing so.

**Severity: Low.** Reads of `n.id` fall back to the key only when no `id` is stored, so the
divergence is visible to queries that filter or return `n.id`.

**Fix (QL-06):** a write may not break the `id == key` correspondence of a node that has it. The
engine enforces this at its write choke points, so every API gets it. In `rewrite_wal_dense_inner`
a `SetProp` of `id` to anything but the node's key is a `QueryError` ("cannot change the key
property `id` of node K; renaming a node is rename_node") when the node's `id` currently equals its
key, judged against the frame's own earlier writes; in `prepare_remove_prop` a removal of `id` from
such a node is refused ("cannot REMOVE the key property `id`; renaming a node is rename_node").
Writing the node's own key is accepted. A node whose `id` differs from its key, or that has none
(for example a store ingested with `key_field = "sku"` and an unrelated `id` column), is not
covered: `id` is an ordinary property there, and insert and ingest store whatever `id` a row
carries. An upsert with `on_conflict="replace"` keeps a stored `id` that equals the key when the
supplied props omit it. WAL replay is never checked, so a store that already holds a diverged `id`
opens unchanged, and `SET n.id = <its key>` makes the node a protected one. Rename's own `id`
move is a `SetProp` to the new key and passes. Tests: `crates/core-api/tests/key_property.rs`,
`set_and_remove_of_the_key_property_are_refused_like_the_map_form`,
`a_node_keyed_on_another_field_treats_id_as_an_ordinary_property` and
`key_property_refusals_hold_inside_a_transaction_too` in `crates/core-api/tests/cypher_set_map.rs`,
`http_prop_routes_refuse_an_id_that_is_not_the_key` in `crates/server/tests/http.rs`, and
`bindings/python/tests/test_key_property.py`.

## 129. A plain `MATCH … SET` reads pre-statement values, so updates are lost — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-21 in the QL-09b review. Present in released 0.9.x.

`exec_match_set` projects every SET value as a column of the MATCH read, then writes the columns.
The read runs once, over the state before the statement, so a value that reads a node property
sees the stored value, never what an earlier item or an earlier row of the same statement set.
Two shapes lose an update, silently:

- **Across rows.** A node bound by several rows is incremented once, not once per row.
- **Within a row.** An item that reads a property an earlier item set reads the old value.

Evidence, one probe run on the v0.9.2 tag (`d8637eb`) and on QL-09 (`6d2f2fe`), with a node
`(:C {id: 'c', count: 0, v: 1})` and two `(:D)` nodes:

| Statement | v0.9.2 | 6d2f2fe | openCypher |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MATCH (n:C {id: 'c'}), (m:D) SET n.count = n.count + 1` | `count = 1` | `count = 1` | `count = 2` |
| `MATCH (n:C {id: 'c'}) SET n.v = 10, n.w = n.v + 0` | `w = 1` | `w = 1` | `w = 10` |
| `MATCH (n:C {id: 'c'}), (m:D) SET n += {count: n.count + 10}` | not parseable (`+=` is new in 0.10) | `count = 11` (from 1) | `count = 22` (from 2) |

**Severity: Medium.** The statement succeeds and writes a wrong value. A second `SET` clause in a
plain MATCH statement is a parse error and a bare `SET n.w = n.v` a named error, so only the
forms above are silent.

**Fix (QL-09b, fix round 1):** a SET value that reads the property of a matched node is
evaluated as its row is written, against an overlay of the statement's own writes and then the
store (`row_eval.rs`: `uw_eval`, `Overlay`), through the executor's own evaluator. This covers
`SET n.f = expr` and `SET n += {…}` / `SET n = {…}`. A statement that binds each node once, and
a value that reads no node, keep the column path and are unchanged. A value that reads both a
node and a relationship variable also keeps the column path. Tests:
`a_plain_match_set_reads_the_writes_of_an_earlier_row_for_the_same_node`,
`a_plain_match_set_item_reads_what_an_earlier_item_set` and
`a_plain_match_set_counter_reads_per_row` in `crates/core-api/tests/ql_09b.rs`.

## 130. A batch that creates edges and deletes nodes commits superlinearly — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-21 while timing QL-09b (clauses after UNWIND). A statement that creates nodes and edges and also
deletes nodes in the same batch spent 711 of 815 ms in `commit_batch` at 20k rows, growing faster than the row count;
batches that only create, or only delete, stay linear. The likely cause is the delete path scanning the batch's
pending edges per deleted node (the same shape PF-03a removed from batch validation, row 118).

**DEFERRED to TX-7 (0.10).** TX-7 owns the write latency gates; the fix is to index pending edges by endpoint in the
commit path as PF-03a did in validation.

**Closed, 0.10 (TX-7).** The cause was as guessed: `MutPreview::note_delete_node` filtered the batch's added and
deleted edges with `retain` for every deleted node, and `note_rename_node` rebuilt both sets for every rename. Both sets
are now `EdgeKeys` (`crates/core-api/src/db.rs`), which also index each edge by its endpoints, so a delete or rename
finds the edges at its node in O(log N + edges at the node). Same edges removed and moved as before.
Tests: `db::tests::a_batch_node_delete_visits_only_the_batch_edges_at_that_node` (200 deletes beside 200 batch edges
visit 1 entry; the old walk visited 20,300) and `random_edge_batches_with_deletes_and_renames_agree_with_separate_commits`
in `crates/core-api/tests/batch_vs_commits.rs` (300 edge-heavy batches against separate commits, live, reopened and
`open_at`). Timed by `bench_create_edges_and_delete_batch_is_linear` (release, `--ignored`; median of 5 per size,
3 interleaved runs, load 8.1–8.4, provisional):

| One batch: N node pairs, N edges, N deletes | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| 5k rows | 64–95 ms | 47–54 ms |
| 20k rows | 586–666 ms | 179–201 ms |
| 20k / 5k | 6.6–9.4× | 3.4–4.3× |

## 131. DISTINCT and grouping keys treated every list and map as one value — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-21 while building QL-07 (named paths, which render as maps). Present in released 0.9.x.

`group_key_normalize` converts a value to the `ValueKey` that `RETURN DISTINCT`, `count(DISTINCT …)`,
`GROUP BY`-style grouping and `UNION` dedup hash on. It numeric-unified `Int` and `Float` and handed every
other value to `ValueKey::from_value`, which has no key for a list, map or duration and returns `None` — the same
`None` a null gets. So every list in a column shared the null group, and so did every map.

Evidence, one probe on the v0.9.2 tag (`d8637eb`) and on `v0.10/bundle-7` (`5268618`), 100 000 nodes
`(:P {tags: [i % 1000, 'x']})`:

| Statement | v0.9.2 | bundle-7 | openCypher |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MATCH (n:P) RETURN DISTINCT n.tags` | 1 row | 1 row | 1000 rows |
| `MATCH (n:P) RETURN n.tags, count(*) AS c` | 1 row | 1 row | 1000 rows |
| `UNWIND [[1],[2],[1]] AS x RETURN DISTINCT x` | not probed | 1 row | 2 rows |

**Severity: Medium.** The statement succeeds and returns too few rows, with the counts of the merged groups.

**Closed by QL-07 (0.10).** The key of a list or map is a canonical byte string built recursively with the
scan's equality (`values_equal`): `Int` and `Float` meet when the `Float` holds the `Int` exactly, the two zeros
meet, an `Int` past 2^53 keeps an exact key, a `DateTime` is its UTC instant, and a null is equal to a null (grouping
treats nulls as one group). The same number rule now applies to a number used directly as a key, which merged `0`
with `-0.0` never and `Int`s past 2^53 always: `[0, -0.0]` is one row, `[2^53, 2^53 + 1]` two. Tests:
`nested_keys_follow_the_scans_equality`, `top_level_keys_agree_with_nested_ones`,
`distinct_and_grouping_tell_lists_and_maps_apart` (`crates/core-query/src/cypher/tests/ql_07.rs`).

Residual: the scan's `=` is not transitive across `Int` and `Float` past 2^53 (`Int(2^53 + 1) = Float(2^53)` is
true while `Int(2^53 + 1) = Int(2^53)` is false), so no single key can match it in every case; the key follows the
exact-representable rule above.

## 132. A variable-length hop ignored its destination's label and properties — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-21 by QL-07 (named paths): `MATCH (a)-[:KNOWS*1..2]->(b:Person {id: 'dee'})` returned paths ending
anywhere. The planner built `VarExpand` with no destination filter (its comment said so), and the planner kept
variable-length hops left to right for that reason, so only the start node's filter was safe.

Confirmed on v0.9.2 (`d8637eb`), built in a scratch worktree, over `a -> b (Person, active) -> c (Person, inactive) ->
d (Robot, active)`:

```text
MATCH (a {id: 'a'})-[*1..3]->(b:Person {active: true}) RETURN b ORDER BY b   => b, c, d
MATCH (a {id: 'a'})-[*1..3]->(b:Person) RETURN b ORDER BY b                  => b, c, d
MATCH (a {id: 'a'})-[*1..3]->(b {active: true}) RETURN b ORDER BY b          => b, c, d
MATCH (a {id: 'a'})-[*1..3]->(b) RETURN b ORDER BY b                         => b, c, d
```

The correct answers are `b`; `b, c`; `b, d`; `b, c, d`.

The same v0.9.2 build refused every `OPTIONAL MATCH` with a variable-length hop, whether or not it matched and with or
without a path name, where the answer is the matching rows or one row with the optional variables null:

```text
MATCH (a {id: 'a'}) OPTIONAL MATCH (a)-[*1..3]->(b) RETURN a, b
  => query error: execute: unsupported op inside OPTIONAL MATCH inner plan: VarExpand { .. }
MATCH (a {id: 'd'}) OPTIONAL MATCH (a)-[:R*1..3]->(b:Person) RETURN a, b
  => query error: execute: unsupported op inside OPTIONAL MATCH inner plan: VarExpand { .. }
```

The planner accepted the clause; the executor that runs an `OPTIONAL MATCH` body had no `VarExpand` arm. It is recorded
here rather than in its own row because the fix is in the same op.

Writes were affected too: `MATCH … SET` and `MATCH … DETACH DELETE` run the same plan. Confirmed on v0.9.2 by QL-RU,
over the graph of `crates/core-api/tests/ql_vl.rs` (`a -> b (Person, active) -> c (Person, inactive) -> d (Robot) ->
e`, `a -> f (Person)`, `e -LIVES-> g`):

```text
MATCH (a {id: 'a'})-[*1..3]->(b:Robot) SET b.hit = true
MATCH (n) WHERE n.hit = true RETURN n ORDER BY n                          => b, c, d, f   (correct: d)
MATCH (a {id: 'a'})-[*1..3]->(b:Person {active: false}) DETACH DELETE b
MATCH (n) RETURN n ORDER BY n                                             => a, e, g      (correct: a, b, d, e, f, g)
```

The DETACH DELETE removed every reachable node instead of the one inactive `Person`: data loss.
`a_write_lands_only_on_the_filtered_destination` in `crates/core-api/tests/ql_vl.rs` pins both (added by QL-RU).

**CLOSED by QL-VL (0.10).** `VarExpand` carries the destination's label and inline properties and checks them on the
node each walk ends at, never on the nodes it passes through. A `shortestPath` destination's label and properties are
rechecked on the bound node before the search. `OPTIONAL MATCH` with a variable-length hop runs: it returns the
matching rows, or one row with the optional variables null when nothing matches, with and without a path name. Tests: `crates/core-query/src/cypher/tests/ql_vl.rs`
(every hop form, incoming and undirected, mid-pattern, two variable-length hops in a row, `OPTIONAL MATCH`, named path,
relationship-list variable, parameters, aggregates, a bound destination, `shortestPath`, index against scan, and a
randomized differential over 150 graphs against a brute-force walk) and `crates/core-api/tests/ql_vl.rs` (live, WAL
replay, snapshotted, reopened, indexed, many commits).

## 133. WHERE evaluated NOT two-valued: `NOT (null)` kept the row — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-21 in QL-BX, which made predicates values and so put a three-valued projection
beside the WHERE evaluator. Present in released 0.9.x.

`eval_expr` read a comparison with a null side as `false` and then applied `NOT`, `AND` and `OR`
to booleans. openCypher evaluates the predicate three-valued and keeps a row only when it is true,
so `NOT (null)` is null and drops the row. `AND` and `OR` gave the same answer both ways; only a
`NOT` over a null (directly, or through an `AND` / `OR` whose result was null) differed, and it
always kept rows openCypher drops.

Evidence, one probe run on the v0.9.2 tag (`d8637eb`, built in a scratch worktree that was then
removed) and on QL-BX, with `(:P {id: 'a', x: 1})`, `(:P {id: 'b', x: 2})` and `(:P {id: 'c'})`,
each as `MATCH (n:P) WHERE … RETURN n.key`:

| WHERE | v0.9.2 | QL-BX | openCypher |
|---|---|---|---|
| `NOT n.x = 1` | `b`, `c` | `b` | `b` |
| `NOT n.missing = 1` | `a`, `b`, `c` | none | none |
| `NOT (n.x = 1 AND n.missing = 2)` | `a`, `b`, `c` | `b` | `b` |
| `NOT (n.missing = 1 OR false)` | `a`, `b`, `c` | none | none |
| `NOT n.x IN [1]` | `b`, `c` | `b` | `b` |

**Severity: Medium.** The query succeeds and returns extra rows. Every `MATCH … WHERE NOT …` over a
property some nodes lack is affected, and so is a write statement's `WHERE` (`MATCH … WHERE NOT …
SET` wrote nodes it should not have).

**Fix (QL-BX, ruling round):** `eval_expr` is now `eval_predicate(..) == Some(true)`
(`crates/core-query/src/cypher/predicate.rs`), the one three-valued evaluator a projection uses.
Every filter path goes through it: the staged filter, the pull path with its fused scan+filter
under a `LIMIT`, aggregate streaming, `OPTIONAL MATCH … WHERE`, `CASE WHEN`, a comprehension's
`WHERE`, and the write-statement `RETURN`'s `CASE WHEN`. Index lookups only take top-level
`AND` equalities, which the change does not affect. `IN` still reads its elements lazily, as the old
path did, so an element it does not need cannot raise an error. Tests:
`where_is_three_valued_under_not_and_and_or` (staged, `LIMIT` and indexed views agree),
`case_when_and_a_comprehension_filter_are_three_valued_too`,
`where_keeps_exactly_the_rows_a_projection_calls_true` and the proptest
`where_keeps_the_rows_where_the_projection_is_true` in
`crates/core-query/src/cypher/tests/ql_bx.rs`, and `a_write_where_drops_a_row_whose_not_is_null` in
`crates/core-api/tests/cypher_bool_exprs.rs`.

## 134. Relationship uniqueness did not span a MATCH clause — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-21 by the QL-VL review's external differential: 4,673 of 30,000 random mid-pattern cases differed
from openCypher. openCypher binds no relationship twice within one MATCH clause, across all its hops and all its
comma-separated parts, and separate clauses do not share the rule. The engine had two faults. A variable-length hop
stored only a hop count in the row, so its edges were neither checked against the earlier hops nor visible to the later
ones. A fixed hop checked against every relationship already in the row, so an edge bound by an earlier clause (or by
the `MATCH` before an `OPTIONAL MATCH`) was refused.

Confirmed on v0.9.2 (`d8637eb`), built in a scratch worktree, over the single edge `n3 -X-> n1`:

```text
MATCH (a)<-[r1]-(b)-[r2:X]->(c) RETURN a, b, c                 => []               (correct)
MATCH (a)<-[*1..1]-(b)-[:X]->(c) RETURN a, b, c                => [n1, n3, n1]     (correct: [])
MATCH (a)-[*1]->(b)-[*1]-(c) RETURN a, b, c                    => [n3, n1, n3]     (correct: [])
MATCH (a)-[r]->(b)-[*1]-(c) RETURN a, b, c                     => [n3, n1, n3]     (correct: [])
MATCH (a)-[*1]->(b), (c)-[s]->(d) RETURN a, b, c, d            => [n3, n1, n3, n1] (correct: [])
MATCH (a)-[r]->(b) MATCH (c)-[s]->(d) RETURN a, b, c, d        => []               (correct: [n3, n1, n3, n1])
MATCH (a)-[r]->(b) OPTIONAL MATCH (a)-[s]->(x) RETURN a, b, x  => [n3, n1, null]   (correct: [n3, n1, n1])
MATCH (a)<-[*1..1]-(b)-[:X]->(c) SET c.hit = true              => sets n1          (correct: sets nothing)
```

and over `a -R-> b -R-> c -R-> d`:

```text
MATCH (a {id: 'b'})<-[*1..2]-(x)-[:R]->(y) RETURN x, y         => [a, b]           (correct: [])
MATCH (a {id: 'b'})<-[*1..2]-(x)-[:R]->(y) DETACH DELETE y
MATCH (n) RETURN n ORDER BY n                                  => a, c, d          (correct: a, b, c, d)
```

The DETACH DELETE removed `b`, which no correct match reaches: data loss. Named paths (`p = …`) did not parse on
v0.9.2; on 0.10 before this fix, `MATCH p = (a)<-[*1..1]-(b)-[:X]->(c)` gave `p.length = 2` over one relationship.

**CLOSED by QL-RU (0.10).** The planner gives each MATCH clause one uniqueness scope (`Pattern.continues_match` marks
the second and later comma-separated parts; an `OPTIONAL MATCH` is its own scope). Every `Expand`, `VarExpand` and
`ShortestPath` lists in `distinct_from` the relationship bindings of the earlier hops of its clause, and skips any edge
they hold. A variable-length or shortest-path hop that a later hop of its clause must avoid keeps its walk in the hidden
trail variable QL-07 introduced, so the later hop can read it. Relationships from other clauses are no longer refused, and a relationship variable an earlier clause bound is joined on
(`MATCH (x)-[r]->(y) MATCH (a)-[r]->(b)` gives one row per edge, `a = x`, `b = y`); the base skipped `r`'s own edge and
bound `r` again to every other edge. The check is pruned by type at plan time: a hop lists only the earlier bindings
whose relationship types can overlap its own (an untyped hop overlaps every hop; directions are never pruned), and a
walk keeps its edges only when a later hop of its clause can overlap it, so `[:KNOWS*]->()-[:WORKS_AT]->` costs
nothing. Naming one relationship variable for two hops of one clause is a named error.
Tests: `crates/core-query/src/cypher/tests/ql_ru.rs` (both hop orders, two variable-length hops, comma-separated parts
including a reversed part, separate clauses and `WITH`, `OPTIONAL MATCH`, named paths, relationship-list variables,
undirected self-loops, parameters, `shortestPath`, the aggregate, grouped, `LIMIT` and staged executors, and the plan)
and `crates/core-api/tests/ql_ru.rs` (`MATCH … SET` and `DETACH DELETE` through `query_write` and a transaction; live,
WAL replay, snapshotting, reopened, indexed and many commits; and a randomized differential against a reference matcher
over every hop form, self-loops, comma-separated parts, separate clauses, `OPTIONAL MATCH`, named paths, parameters, an
indexed store and the write path). At 2,500 graphs the differential runs 20,000 read cases and 7,543 writes: the base
gives 1,757 wrong reads and 452 wrong writes, the fix gives none.
## 135. IN flattened list-valued elements — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-21 in the QL-BX review, by an independent openCypher evaluator run over the
610 corpus queries QL-BX changed (6 of them came out wrong because of this). Present in released
0.9.x.

The parser read `x IN [a, b]` as a `Vec` of operands and `x IN $l` as a one-element `Vec`, so the
two had the same tree, and the evaluator could not tell a list operand from a list element. It
expanded every list-valued element: `x IN [[1, 2], 3]` was `x IN [1, 2, 3]`. openCypher compares
each element whole, so `[1, 2] IN [[1, 2]]` is true, `1 IN [[1]]` is false and `null IN [[]]` is
null.

Evidence, one probe run on the v0.9.2 tag (`d8637eb`, built in a scratch worktree that was then
removed) and on QL-BX, with `(:P {id: 'a', x: 1, tags: [1, 2]})`, `(:P {id: 'b', x: 3, tags: [3]})`
and `$l = $t = [1, 2]`, each as `MATCH (n:P) WHERE … RETURN n.key`:

| WHERE | v0.9.2 | QL-BX | openCypher |
|---|---|---|---|
| `n.x IN [$l]` | `a` | none | none |
| `n.x IN [$l, 3]` | `a`, `b` | `b` | `b` |
| `n.tags IN [$t]` | none | `a` | `a` |
| `n.tags IN [$t, 0]` | none | `a` | `a` |
| `n.x IN $l` | `a` | `a` | `a` |

**Severity: Medium.** The query succeeds and returns wrong rows, too many or too few. Only a written
IN list with a list-valued element is affected; `IN $l` and `IN [1, 2]` were right.

**Fix (QL-BX, fix round 1):** `Expr::In { expr, list }` now holds one `list: Operand`, parsed as an
additive expression (`IN [a, b]`, `IN $l`, `IN [1] + [2]`). A written list with a non-constant
element stays `Operand::ListLit` and is read lazily, element by element, each compared whole
(`predicate::in_list`); a constant list is borrowed. Tests: `in_does_not_flatten_list_valued_elements`
and `in_reads_its_elements_lazily_as_where_always_did` in `crates/core-query/src/cypher/tests/ql_bx.rs`.

## 136. Index declarations can be lost in an archive crash window on non-V11 stores — DEFERRED

Found on 2026-10-22 during TY-12. Present in released 0.9.x.

A history-preserving snapshot (`archive_wal`) writes the snapshot, renames the live WAL to
`wal.<n>.archive`, then writes the minimal baseline WAL that re-declares the store's live declarations
(`crates/core-api/src/db.rs`, the `archive_wal` branch of the snapshot path). Row 22 moved the baseline
write to directly after the rename, so no ordinary I/O error can land between them, and the V10 stamp
recovers the multiplicity opt-in on open. A crash between the two filesystem calls still leaves a store
whose live WAL is gone and whose snapshot does not record its equality index declarations: the store
reopens without them.

**Severity: Low.** It needs a crash in the window between one rename and one atomic write, and the lost
declaration can be made again; no data is lost.

**Closed on V11 stores by TY-12.** A V11 snapshot carries the store's constraints, equality and range
indexes and the multiplicity opt-in in section 18 (`docs/format-stability.md`), so the archived WAL is
not needed to recover them. V9 bytes must stay byte-identical to what 0.9.2 writes, so a V9 or V10
snapshot cannot carry them and those stores keep the window.

**DEFERRED.** The fix for a non-V11 store is a format change, which a store takes by moving to V11.

## 137. The first edge write after a fold copies the topology overlay — DEFERRED to 0.11

Found on 2026-10-22 by the review of TX-7 (ledger row 126). The topology overlay (`Topology`: per edge
type, per node, its adjacency, plus tombstones) is shared with the reader's fold through an `Arc` like the
other store structures, and is not yet copy-on-write in pieces. TX-7's fix round 1 stopped commits that
write no edge from copying it (`GraphMut` holds it as `CowMut`), but the first commit after a fold that
does write an edge copies it whole. On a store with no snapshot the overlay is every edge. Measured
before that fix, as the first plain commit after a fold on a 1M-node store with 2M edges: about 77 ms
(`fold_costs` with `EDGES=2`, writer CPU time, load 8.2, one run), against about 0.3 ms without edges.
On a snapshotted store the overlay holds only what was written since the snapshot.

**DEFERRED to 0.11.** The fix is the same structural sharing as row 126: adjacency lists and tombstone
maps in `cow` containers, so an edge write copies its node's list and the path to it.

## 138. A different or rewound store on a reused backup prefix destroyed its own backup or built a mixed restore point — CLOSED (HO-10)

Found on 2026-10-22 by the HO-08 review. Introduced in 0.10 by HO-07 (continuous backup) and never
released. HO-08 made it automatic: a waiting instance takes over a prefix once its lease expires.

The manifest named no store, manifests were keyed and ordered by `commit_seq` alone, and resume
seeded "already uploaded" archives from `LATEST` by archive number and length. When a store with a
lower commit count (a new volume, a PITR restore, a replaced machine) took over a prefix:

- **Retention deleted its backup.** Its manifest sorted oldest and was dropped as surplus. The
  snapshot and archives only that manifest named went with it. `LATEST` then named a missing
  manifest, and health stayed OK.
- **Manifests were overwritten** when the two stores reached the same `commit_seq`.
- **A mixed restore point verified.** An archive with the same number and length as one in the
  prefix was not uploaded. The new manifest listed the other store's archive, with that archive's
  CRC, so it verified and restored the wrong history.

**Severity: High.** Silent loss of backups, and a restore point that restores another store's data.

**Status: CLOSED (HO-10).** Every restore gives the restored store a new lineage. HO-08 closed takeover by a different store, and a rewound store that holds fewer commits than `LATEST`. HO-09 (`mushroomdb restore --from`) gives every restore it makes a new lineage. HO-10 (restore on boot) does the same, and its store continues the prefix it came from without touching the parent chain.

**Fix so far (HO-08):**

- The first backup of a store gives it a random lineage (`backup.lineage`, carried as a side file).
- Manifest version 2 records the lineage in the manifest and in every file and segment entry.
  `Manifest::from_json` refuses a manifest that mixes lineages.
- Resume refuses the prefix when `LATEST` is another lineage's, when the store has no lineage but
  the prefix has restore points, or when the store holds fewer commits than `LATEST`. On refusal
  the worker uploads nothing, deletes nothing, reports unhealthy with the reason, and releases the
  lease.
- Archives are reused only on a matching number, length and CRC.
- Retention reads only the lineage's manifests and deletes only objects of its generations. It
  never deletes the manifest just written, or `LATEST`'s target.
- Immutable objects are written with `If-None-Match`, and manifests and `LATEST` with `If-Match`,
  so the bucket refuses a stale writer.

Tests: `crates/backup/tests/lineage.rs`, `manifest::tests::a_restore_point_mixing_two_lineages_does_not_parse`.

**Fix so far (HO-09):**

- `mushroomdb restore --from` writes a new random `backup.lineage` into every store it restores:
  the newest restore point, an older generation by `--at <commit>`, and a point in time by
  `--at <timestamp>`. The source's lineage file is checked against its manifest and then replaced.
- So a restored store pointed back at its source prefix is refused as another store's, even after
  it has written more commits than the prefix's newest restore point: it uploads nothing and
  deletes nothing there.
- The other existing restore path, `serve --restore-from <dir>` (`restore_if_empty`, also the
  Python binding's), copies the files `GraphDb::backup_to` writes, which do not include
  `backup.lineage`. A store it restores has no lineage, so the source prefix refuses it too, and
  its first backup to a new prefix mints a new one.

Tests: `crates/backup/tests/restore.rs`
(`every_restore_is_a_new_lineage_that_never_resumes_into_the_source`, on an in-memory and an
on-disk bucket), and `s3_restore_latest_and_a_point_in_time` in `crates/backup/tests/minio.rs`.

**Fix (HO-10):**

- Restore on boot (`MUSHROOMDB_RESTORE_FROM_S3`) writes a new random `backup.lineage` into the store it
  restores, and a `backup.parent` record naming the source lineage, the manifest and its commit count.
- The worker continues the prefix for such a store only while `LATEST` still names exactly that
  manifest of exactly that lineage. If the prefix moved on after the restore, it is refused like any
  other lineage.
- It never reuses the parent's archives, writes only its own generations, does not write a manifest
  key the parent holds (the same commit count), and retention never deletes an object another
  lineage's manifest names. Side files are keyed by content, so the two lineages can share one.
- Tests: `crates/backup/tests/boot.rs`
  (`a_boot_restore_continues_the_prefix_as_a_new_lineage_without_touching_the_parent`: boot-restore,
  write, ship across a truncating snapshot with retention keeping one; every parent object is
  unchanged, and the parent's newest restore point restores the source; `a_prefix_that_moved_on_since_the_boot_restore_is_refused`;
  `only_a_boot_restore_record_continues_the_prefix`), and `restore_on_boot_empty_dir` in
  `crates/cli/tests/serve_backup.rs` (MinIO, through `serve`).

**Remaining (documented in `docs/site/deployment.md`):**

- A copy of a store made outside the restore commands (its directory copied by hand) keeps its
  lineage. If it is older than the prefix's newest restore point and then writes more commits than
  that restore point before backup is enabled, it passes the commit check. Its own restore points
  verify, but the prefix's newer history from the other branch is not removed. Documented in
  `docs/site/deployment.md`.

## 139. A filtered scan with LIMIT missed every node in the snapshot base — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-22 by TY-10 (range planner), whose no-index differential disagreed with the scan on this shape alone.
`MATCH (n:L) WHERE n.f <op> <literal> RETURN … LIMIT k` with no ORDER BY takes the pull path's fused scan-and-filter:
it resolves the property column once and compares inline. The handle it resolved, `ColumnsView::column`, wrapped the
overlay `ColumnStore`'s column (and, from 0.10, the layer above it) and never the archived base a snapshot leaves
mapped; its doc comment said so and told callers that need the base to use `get()`. The fused path did not. After a
snapshot every value lives in the base until it is written again, so every untouched node read as "no value" and was
dropped, with no error. Every other read (`get()`, the unfused filter, a projection) consults the base, so the same
filter written any other way answered correctly.

Which comparisons were affected: all six. The fused arm fires on any label scan whose next operation is a lone
`Prop <op> Lit` filter on that scan's variable, with SKIP or without. On 0.9.x the planner (`fold_where_equalities`,
`crates/core-query/src/cypher/plan.rs`) lifts a property equality into a lookup only on the **first** scan's variable,
so `MATCH (n:L) WHERE n.f = 3 … LIMIT k` was right, and `MATCH (m:M), (n:L) WHERE n.f = 3 … LIMIT k` and
`MATCH (m:M) MATCH (n:L) WHERE n.f = 3 … LIMIT k` were not. This row said at first, on the 0.9.3 branch, that `=` was
never affected; the 0.9.3 review showed it was, on the released 0.9.2 binary:

| Query on 0.9.2 (`a`, `b` `{val: 3}` and `m:M`, `snapshot`, `c` `{val: 3}`) | Got | Correct |
|---|---|---|
| `MATCH (n:L) WHERE n.val = 3 RETURN n.key AS k LIMIT 10` | a b c | a b c |
| `MATCH (n:L) WHERE n.val <> 9 … LIMIT 10` | c | a b c |
| `MATCH (m:M), (n:L) WHERE n.val = 3 … LIMIT 10` | c | a b c |
| `MATCH (m:M) MATCH (n:L) WHERE n.val = 3 … LIMIT 10` | c | a b c |
| `MATCH (n:L) WHERE n.val <> 9 … SKIP 1 LIMIT 10` | (nothing) | b c |
| `MATCH (m:M), (n:L) WHERE n.val = 3 …` (no LIMIT) | a b c | a b c |

Present since 0.2.0, when the V8 mapped base arrived (`f13187b6`), on every handle that reads a base: the live handle
after `snapshot()`, a reopened store, `SharedDb` readers folded and unfolded, `open_at` / `query_at`, role and
namespace masks, and a transaction. Confirmed on v0.9.2 (`d8637eb`), built in a scratch directory, with
`the_reported_shape_sees_the_snapshot_base`:

```text
insert a {val: 1}, b {val: 2}; snapshot(); insert c {val: 4}
MATCH (n:L) WHERE n.val <> 3 RETURN n.key AS k LIMIT 5   => c        (correct: a, b, c)
```

**CLOSED by QL-FS (0.10).** `ColumnsView::column` returns a `ViewColumn` that reads exactly what `get()` reads, in
the same order: the layer, the overlay, the overlay's tombstones, then the base column, with the field looked up once
per handle. A `Mixed` base column is decoded once per handle (from the `MixedCache` when the view has one).
`ViewColumn::get` now returns a `ValueRef`; the fused loop calls `ViewColumn::test`, which hands the layer's or
overlay's value to the comparison unwrapped.

**CLOSED in 0.9.3** by a hand port (0.9.x has no layer). There `ColumnsView::view_column` returns the `ViewColumn`
(overlay, tombstones, base, sharing `get()`'s base decoder `base_value`), and `ColumnsView::column`, the overlay-only
handle, keeps its 0.9.2 signature so a caller of the published `mushroomdb-storage` still compiles, and is deprecated;
the engine no longer calls it. Read side only: no byte a snapshot or the WAL writes changed. When 0.9.3 was merged into
0.10, 0.10 kept its `column` (not deprecated) and gained `view_column` as the same handle under the 0.9.3 name.

Tests: `crates/core-api/tests/fused_scan_base.rs` — every comparison operator against Int, Float, Str, Bool, List,
Map and null literals (and a second Int), each in four forms (`MATCH (n:L) … LIMIT`, the same with `SKIP 1`,
`MATCH (m:M), (n:L)` and `MATCH (m:M) MATCH (n:L)`); typed and mixed base columns; nodes in the base, in the delta,
overwritten, deleted and with the property removed after the snapshot; a second snapshot; live and reopened handles,
namespace and role masks, `open_at` and `query_at`, `SharedDb` readers unfolded and folded, a transaction; the
`=`-on-a-later-scan and SKIP shapes from the table above (`an_equality_on_a_later_scan_sees_the_snapshot_base`); and a
randomized differential, fused against unfused in all four forms, over 16 seeds of 70 random insert / overwrite /
remove / delete / snapshot / reopen steps, one of them a burst of `FOLD_EVERY_K + 1` writes so a reader folds inside
the walk, through the handle and a reader — and `v8::seam::tests::the_column_handle_reads_what_get_reads` (typed
columns, `Vector` among them, `Mixed`, and a layer). On 0.10 the seven QL-FS tests fail on the QL-FS base commit; the
eighth fails with the base read disabled. On 0.9.3 all eight fail on `1200cdfe` (0.9's copy has no transaction and no
list, map or `null` literal, which 0.9's WHERE does not parse). The review of 0.9.3 ran a wider differential,
3,507,840 comparisons over 64 seeds, with no failure.

Timing, 0.10 (QL-FS), release build, 100k `L` nodes, `WHERE … LIMIT 10` that scans the whole label, median of 51
runs, each binary run five times interleaved: with no snapshot (overlay only) unchanged, Int 0.599 → 0.582 ms and Str
0.921 → 0.919 ms (medians of the five runs); after a snapshot, 0.96 ms (Int) and 3.2 ms (Str) for the correct rows,
where the base answered no rows in 0.18 ms, against 7.1 ms and 12.7 ms for the unfused twin of each.

Timing, 0.9.3, provisional (release build, one macOS machine also running other work, 100k `L` nodes with an Int and
a Str property, median of 21 runs):

| Query | no snapshot, before | no snapshot, after | after a snapshot, before | after a snapshot, after |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WHERE n.val <> 3 … LIMIT 1000000` (90,000 rows) | 13.6 ms | 13.3–15.1 ms | 0.10 ms, **0 rows** | 12.1–13.4 ms |

The no-snapshot path did not move beyond run-to-run noise; after a snapshot there is no correct "before" to compare
with.

## 140. A role-token or read-key transaction cannot write — DEFERRED to 0.11

Found on 2026-10-22 by the review of TX-4. `Transaction` (TX-1) buffers writes as `BatchOp`s and commits
them through the compare-and-set path with no role in it; the role-scoped write path
(`query_write_authz`, `submit_batch_authz`) checks scope and visibility under the write guard and has no
precondition form. TX-4 therefore gives a role token or `read`-tier key a read-only transaction on the
snapshot it began on, with every read going through the role's mask, and answers a write with 403 (the
key's own refusal text for a `read` key, `role-bound token: writes are not permitted inside a
transaction` for a role token, including one whose role carries a write scope). Its commit writes nothing
and returns the snapshot's commit number. Nothing is widened. A Bolt `BEGIN` for such an identity has the
same engine and the same gap. `docs/site/http.md` says so in the Transactions section.

**DEFERRED to 0.11.** The fix is an authz-aware commit: `submit_batch_cas` taking the role, checking the
scope and visibility of each buffered op under the same guard as the preconditions.

## 141. `serve --tls-cert/--tls-key` panicked building the TLS config: two rustls crypto providers compiled, none installed — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-22 by TX-16 (the Bolt listener, which serves the same certificate) and confirmed
by its review. Introduced in the 0.10 cycle when HO-07 and HO-01 met; never released.

- HO-01 builds the published image with `--features embed-ui,tls` (`Dockerfile`). axum-server's
  `tls-rustls` feature turns on rustls's `aws-lc-rs` provider.
- HO-07 added the backup client: `mushroomdb-backup → object_store → reqwest/hyper-rustls`, which
  turns on rustls's `ring` provider in the CLI's normal dependency graph.
- With both features on, `CryptoProvider::from_crate_features()` is `None`, and nothing in the
  binary installs a provider. `RustlsConfig::from_pem_file` calls `ServerConfig::builder()`, which
  panics ("Could not automatically determine the process-level CryptoProvider", rustls 0.23.43
  `crypto/mod.rs:249`) inside the spawned HTTP task. The image could not serve native TLS.
- CI's `cargo test -p mushroomdb-server --features tls --test tls` stayed green because that test
  installs ring itself, and nothing in CI built the TLS CLI.
- Not in any release: at v0.9.2 ring reaches only the server crate's dev-dependencies
  (`cargo tree --locked -p mushroomdb-cli --features tls -e normal,build -i ring` prints nothing),
  and neither `release.yml` nor the `Dockerfile` built `tls`.

**Severity: High** for 0.10: native TLS, a documented deployment path, failed at start.

**Fix (TX-16):** one helper, `server::load_server_config` (`crates/server/src/tls.rs`, contract C4),
builds the config with `builder_with_provider`: the process's installed provider, else aws-lc-rs.
