HTTP (with h2/http1.1 ALPN added) and Bolt both use it. It installs nothing, so the S3 client keeps
its own provider choice. Evidence:
- `crates/server/tests/tls_no_provider.rs`, its own test binary with no provider installed: asserts
  that none is installed, builds the config, and serves `GET /health` over HTTPS. Red on `e18fdfc`,
  where the same steps panic in `from_pem_file`.
- `serve_over_tls_answers_https_and_bolt_over_tls` in `crates/cli/tests/serve_bolt.rs` (feature
  `tls`): the real binary, which installs no provider, serves HTTPS and Bolt over TLS.
- CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) builds the CLI with `--features tls` and runs both, plus the Bolt
  listener's TLS test.

## 142. A key revoked by a `keys.json` reload keeps working on an open Bolt connection — DEFERRED to 0.11

Found on 2026-10-22 when TX-17 documented Bolt's scoped access. Introduced in the 0.10 cycle where HO-04
(`keys.json` with a `SIGHUP` reload) met TX-16 (the Bolt listener); never released.

A Bolt connection authenticates once, at `HELLO`, and keeps the `Identity` it resolved for its whole life.
A `SIGHUP` reload replaces the key set both listeners read, so a new `HELLO` with a removed key is refused,
and HTTP, which resolves the key on every request, refuses it on the next one. A connection that
authenticated before the reload keeps running statements with the revoked key until the client closes
it. A change to the role a credential names is not affected: a scoped statement resolves the role's mask
when it runs. `docs/site/bolt.md` (Scoped access, "Revoking a credential") says so and tells the
operator to close such connections or restart.

**DEFERRED to 0.11.** The fix is for a reload to reach open connections: each statement (or each
`BEGIN`/auto-commit `RUN`) checks that its key is still in the current key set, or the reload closes the
connections whose key it removed.

## 143. No write result counted the relationships a statement created — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-22 by the QL-12 review. Introduced in the 0.10 cycle by QL-09b (`UNWIND … CREATE/MERGE`
and relationship `MERGE`); never released.

- `UNWIND … MATCH (a), (b) CREATE (a)-[:K]->(b)` created the edge and reported `created = 0`, and a
  `MERGE` that created only a relationship reported nothing.
- `created` counting nodes only is correct under its documented meaning (nodes inserted, as on 0.9.x);
  what was missing was a relationship counter beside it.
- On v0.9.2 (`git show v0.9.2:crates/core-api/src/db.rs`, `exec_create`) neither `UNWIND` writes nor
  relationship `MERGE` existed. Its one edge-creating path, `CREATE (a:L {…})-[:K]->(b:L {…})`, reported
  `created = 2` with nothing for the edge.

**Severity: Low**: a missing count, no wrong data.

**Fix (QL-CX):** the write row gains a fourth column, `relationships_created`, after `created`,
`properties_set` and `deleted`, which keep their positions and meanings. It is filled on every create
path: `CREATE`, `MERGE`, `UNWIND … CREATE/MERGE`, `MATCH … CREATE/MERGE`, and `CREATE` and `MERGE` inside a
transaction. Bolt returns it as a field of the write result. Documented in `docs/site/query.md` (write
result columns). Evidence: `row_143_every_create_path_counts_its_relationships` and
`row_143_a_transaction_counts_its_relationships_too` in `crates/core-api/tests/ql_cx.rs`, plus
`relationships_created` assertions beside the `created` ones in `cypher_merge_edge.rs`,
`cypher_writes.rs` and `ql_09b.rs`.

## 144. MATCH-driven writes were refused inside a transaction — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-22 by the QL-CX review. Introduced in the 0.10 cycle (TX-1, QL-09b, QL-CX); never released.

- Inside a transaction (Rust `begin_transaction`, HTTP `/tx`, Bolt `BEGIN`, so every driver's managed
  write transaction) `MATCH … CREATE` / `MATCH … MERGE`, `UNWIND … <write>`, `MATCH … SET n = {…}` /
  `SET n += {…}`, `MATCH … SET … RETURN` and `MATCH … REMOVE … RETURN` failed with
  `not supported in mushroomdb 0.10 inside a transaction: …`.
- `MATCH (a {id: $a}), (b {id: $b}) CREATE (a)-[:K]->(b)` failed over Bolt `BEGIN`; only auto-commit
  `RUN` ran it.

**Severity: Medium**: a refused statement, no wrong data.

**Fix (TX-MW):** the per-row executor and the `MATCH … SET` / `REMOVE` executors compute their ops over
a `RowSource` (the store, or the transaction's base snapshot with its buffer laid over it), and the
transaction buffers those ops instead of committing them. Evidence: `crates/core-api/tests/tx_mw.rs` (a
randomized differential of the same statements as auto-commit and inside one transaction,
read-your-write, rollback, conflict on the same pair, constraint at commit),
`match_then_create_connects_two_nodes_inside_a_transaction` in `crates/server/tests/http_tx.rs`, and
`begin_match_create_commit_connects_two_nodes` in `crates/bolt/tests/tx.rs`.

## 145. Inside a transaction, MATCH … SET read the state before the statement — CLOSED

Found by TX-MW's differential. Introduced in the 0.10 cycle (TX-1); never released.

- Over four rows matching one node, `MATCH (n {id: 'a'}), (m:Person) SET n.v = n.v + 1` added 1, not 4;
  `SET n.v = 10, n.w = n.v + 0` set `w` from the old `v`. Auto-commit evaluates against the statement's
  own earlier rows and items.

**Severity: Medium**: wrong values.

**Fix (TX-MW):** the transaction computes `MATCH … SET` through the auto-commit executor
(`match_set_ops` over the transaction's `RowSource`). Evidence:
`match_set_reads_its_own_earlier_rows_inside_a_transaction` in `crates/core-api/tests/tx_mw.rs`.

## 146. Inside a transaction, DETACH DELETE counted a shared edge twice — CLOSED

Found by TX-MW's differential. Introduced in the 0.10 cycle (TX-1); never released.

- `MATCH … DETACH DELETE` counted an edge between two nodes the statement deleted once from each end in
  `deleted`; auto-commit counts it once. Data correct.

**Severity: Low**: a wrong counter.

**Fix (TX-MW):** `stage_match_delete_node` counts an incident edge only when neither end was deleted
earlier in the statement. Evidence:
`row_146_an_edge_between_two_deleted_nodes_is_counted_once_in_a_transaction` in
`crates/core-api/tests/tx_mw.rs`.

## 147. DETACH DELETE counted a self-loop as two deleted relationships — CLOSED

Found by the TX-MW review. Present in released 0.9.x (checked against v0.9.2, whose `delete_node`
computes the same sum).

- `delete_node` summed the node's Out and In neighbour lists, and a self-loop is in both, so
  `DeleteReport.manual_edges`, the `deleted` column of `MATCH … DETACH DELETE`, Python `delete_node` /
  `forget` (`manual_edges`) and the MCP task report were one too high per self-loop.

**Severity: Low**: a wrong counter, data correct.

**Fix (TX-MW):** the In list skips the node itself, so a self-loop is counted once on every delete path
(auto-commit, `delete_node`, and inside a transaction). Evidence: `row_147_a_self_loop_is_deleted_once`
in `crates/core-api/tests/tx_mw.rs`.

## 148. Bolt sent a node as its key string and refused a bare relationship — CLOSED

Found by TX-18 (the official-driver gate). Introduced in the 0.10 cycle with the Bolt listener; never
released.

- `RETURN n` sent the node's key string, not a Node structure, and `RETURN r` for a relationship
  failed with `cannot return relationship variable 'r' bare`. `PackStruct::node` existed and was never
  used. The drivers' `record["n"]["name"]`, `record["r"].type` and path idioms failed, and TX-18's
  `streaming` scenario failed with both drivers.

**Severity: Medium**: the common driver idiom read the wrong type.

**Fix (TX-NS):** the engine marks each result column that is a bare node, relationship or path
variable (`ResultSet::column_kind`, worked out from the plan). Inside `with_entity_detail`, which wraps
every Bolt statement, the query entry points attach the element behind each such cell, read from the
view the statement ran on (so a role's mask applies, a relationship to a hidden node never appears,
and a transaction sees its own writes). Bolt sends Node, Relationship and Path in the 5.0 or 4.4 shape;
element ids are `n:<key>` and `r:<type>|<start>|<end>` (escaped). A fixed relationship variable may be
returned bare, carried by `WITH`, grouped on and listed by `RETURN *`; on HTTP and in Python it renders
as the `{type, start, end}` map. A node still renders as its key there. Evidence:
`crates/core-query/src/cypher/tests/tx_ns.rs`, `crates/core-api/tests/tx_ns.rs`,
`crates/bolt/tests/structures.rs`, and TX-18's scripts (`python driver 6.4.1: 12/12`,
`javascript driver 6.2.0: 12/12`).

## 149. A Bolt write carried no counters — CLOSED

Found by TX-18. Introduced in the 0.10 cycle with the Bolt listener; never released.

- A write's closing `SUCCESS` had no `stats`, so `summary.counters` read zero and `contains_updates`
  false for every write. The engine had nothing finer than the write row, whose `deleted` mixes nodes
  and relationships and whose `properties_set` leaves out a new node's properties.

**Severity: Low**: a missing summary, data correct.

**Fix (TX-NS):** every write path records `WriteStats` on its result (auto-commit and in a
transaction, with or without `RETURN`, DDL included); Bolt sends the counters that moved as
`nodes-created`, `nodes-deleted`, `relationships-created`, `relationships-deleted`, `properties-set`,
`labels-added`, `indexes-added`/`-removed` and `constraints-added`/`-removed`. The write row is
unchanged. Evidence: `every_write_statement_reports_exact_stats` and
`a_transaction_reports_the_same_stats_as_auto_commit` in `crates/core-api/tests/tx_ns.rs`,
`a_write_success_carries_its_counters` in `crates/bolt/tests/structures.rs`.

## 150. MERGE did not count its ON CREATE / ON MATCH SET items — CLOSED

Found by QL-PM. Present in released 0.9.x (checked against v0.9.2, whose `exec_merge` builds the write
row with a literal 0 for `properties_set`).

- `MERGE … ON CREATE SET …` and `MERGE … ON MATCH SET …`, node and relationship form, reported
  `properties_set = 0` in the write row, auto-commit and inside a transaction, while `UNWIND … MERGE`
  counted the same items.

**Severity: Low**: a wrong counter, data correct.

**Fix (TX-NS):** the plain `MERGE` paths (`exec_merge`, `exec_merge_edge`, and the transaction's
`stage_merge` / `stage_merge_edge`) count the `ON CREATE` or `ON MATCH` items they apply. Evidence:
`row_150_merge_set_items_are_counted_in_properties_set` in `crates/core-api/tests/tx_ns.rs`.

## 151. Map-form SET with an `id` key and `SET n.id` were reported to disagree — CLOSED (not a defect, pinned)

Reported by the QL-PM review. Introduced in the 0.10 cycle (QL-06, QL-PM) if at all; never released.

- The report: `SET n += $props` / `SET n = $props` (plain, `ON CREATE` / `ON MATCH`, the `UNWIND`
  form) with an `id` key writes an `id` that differs from the node's key, while `SET n.id` is refused
  (the id≡key rule, row 128).
- Checked by QL-RP on the 0.10 branch, auto-commit and inside a transaction: every map form gives the
  answer `SET n.id` gives. On a node whose `id` equals its key all of them refuse a diverging `id` and
  write nothing; on a name-keyed node all of them store `id` as an ordinary property, which is the
  documented rule (row 128, `query.md`).
- v0.9.2 has neither the map forms nor the id≡key rule, so no release had it.

**Severity: Low** (reported inconsistency, not reproduced).

**Closed (QL-RP), no code change:** the parity is pinned by
`row_151_map_forms_and_set_id_give_the_same_answer` in `crates/core-api/tests/ql_rp.rs`.

## 152. Over Bolt, a node inside a map, a mixed list or a computed value is its key — DEFERRED to 0.11

Found by the TX-NS review. Introduced in the 0.10 cycle with TX-NS's graph structures; never released.

A result column carries an entity kind worked out from the plan: a bare node, relationship or path
variable, or a list of one kind of them (`collect(v)`, `[a, b]`, `p.nodes`, `p.relationships`). Bolt
sends those as Node, Relationship and Path structures. A node or relationship anywhere else keeps the
value every other surface renders (a key, a `{type, start, end}` map): in a map built in the query
(`RETURN {who: n}`), in a list that mixes kinds or holds values (`[n, r]`, `[n, 1]`), in a list of lists,
or behind a function or expression (`head(collect(n))`, `coalesce(n, m)`, `CASE … THEN n`). A driver
reads a string or a map there, not a Node. `docs/site/bolt.md` (Nodes, relationships and paths,
"Lists") says so.

**DEFERRED to 0.11.** The fix is a per-value kind rather than a per-column one: the executor tags each
graph element where it is produced, so a renderer can find it at any depth. That is a new value form, or
a parallel tag tree beside the row, and is larger than this unit.

## 154. A retracted rule's saved edge weight came back live, then vanished at the next snapshot — CLOSED

Found by the QL-RP review. Present in released 0.9.x by code identity with v0.9.2 (not run on a
v0.9.2 binary).

- Two weighted rules over one edge type: `ra` (weight `wa`) owns an edge, `rb` (weight `wb`) also
  matches it. A snapshot archives `wa`. When `ra` retracts and `rb` claims the edge, `ra`'s
  `remove_edge` tombstones the key and `rb`'s `EdgeProps::set` cleared the tombstone, so the live read
  showed `wa = 1.0` beside `wb` for a rule that no longer owned the edge.
- The next snapshot's merge (`edge_props_merge_to_data`) emitted the overlay entry alone, so the same
  read then gave `wa = null`: the answer changed across a snapshot.
- v0.9.2 has the same mechanism: `set` removes the tombstone (`core-storage/src/edge_props.rs`),
  `EdgePropsView::get` falls through to the base after the tombstone check (`v8/seam.rs`), the merge
  is byte-identical, and the retract-then-claim path is in `core-rules/src/engine.rs`.

**Severity: Low**: wrong values, needs two weighted rules over one type and an ownership hand-off
after a snapshot.

**Fix (QL-RP):** `EdgeProps::set` keeps a tombstone (the entry is then the edge's whole map), and the
snapshot merge lays an untombstoned overlay entry over the archived one field by field, as every read
does. Evidence: `row_154_a_retracted_rules_weight_does_not_reappear_across_a_snapshot` in
`crates/core-api/tests/ql_rp.rs` (live, after a snapshot, after a reopen).

## 155. Relationship-property writes emit no mutation or subscription event — DEFERRED to 0.11

Found by the QL-RP review (M4). Introduced in the 0.10 cycle with QL-RP; never released.

- A relationship-property write from Cypher — a `CREATE` or `MERGE` relationship map, `SET r.p`,
  `SET r += …`, `SET r = …`, `REMOVE r.p` — is applied as the V11 records `SetEdgePropId` (28) and
  `RemoveEdgePropId` (29). The engine's apply emits no `MutationEvent` and no `DbEvent` for them, since
  neither enum has a variant that names a relationship and a field.
- So `/watch` and `/subscribe` (with `writes: true`) consumers do not see relationship-property
  changes. A `/subscribe` `cypher` query that reads `r.p` is re-run on each commit and does see the
  new value. Node property writes (`prop_set`, `prop_removed`) are unaffected.

**Severity: Low**: a missing notification; the write is durable and every read sees it.

**DEFERRED to 0.11.** The fix is a pair of event variants (`edge_prop_set`, `edge_prop_removed`, with
`edge_type`, `src`, `dst`, `field`) on both streams, emitted where the records are applied, and their
documentation in `docs/site/http.md` and `docs/site/subscriptions.md`.

## 156. `elementId()` refuses anything but a bare node or relationship variable — DEFERRED to 0.11

Allocated by the controller for bundle-18. Introduced in the 0.10 cycle with QL-03e; never released (0.9.x has no
`elementId()`).

- `elementId(n)` and `elementId(r)` on a bound variable return the same string as the Bolt Node / Relationship
  structure's `element_id`.
- An argument that is any other expression yielding a node or relationship is refused:
  `elementId(startNode(r))`, `elementId(endNode(r))`, `elementId(nodes(p)[0])`.

**Severity: Low**: a refusal (`elementId() argument must be a node or relationship variable`), not a wrong
result. Passing the element through `WITH` does not help: `WITH startNode(r) AS a RETURN elementId(a)` is refused
too (``argument `a` is not a node or a relationship``). Binding the node in the pattern itself (`MATCH (a)-[r]->(b)
RETURN elementId(a)`) is the workaround.

**DEFERRED to 0.11.** The fix is to resolve the element id from the evaluated value rather than from the variable
binding, which needs the value to carry its kind (the same per-value tag row 152 needs).

## 157. Aggregates over a bare variable returned null — CLOSED

Found on 2026-10-23 by QL-10's TCK run (Aggregation2 [1]–[12]). `UNWIND [1,2] AS x RETURN max(x)` returned null, as did
`min`, `sum` and `avg`, and the same over a variable carried through `WITH`. `max(n.prop)` worked.

Cause: `update_acc` matched `(AggFunc::Sum | Avg | Min | Max, AggArg::Prop { .. })` and nothing for `AggArg::Var`, so a
row whose argument was a variable reached the catch-all `_ => {}` arm and fed the accumulator nothing. `count`,
`collect`, `stDev`, `stDevP` and the percentiles have their own `Var` handling and were right. `min` and `max` also
kept only values `numeric_val` accepted, so over strings they returned null even through a property.

Confirmed on v0.9.2 (`d8637eb`) by reading `git show v0.9.2:crates/core-query/src/cypher/exec.rs`: its `update_acc`
has the same four `Prop`-only arms (lines 2898, 2908, 2918, 2938), and `UNWIND` (the source of a scalar variable)
exists there, so the defect shipped in 0.9.x. The tag was not built.

**CLOSED by QL-AG (0.10).** One arm handles `Prop` and `Var` for the four aggregates through `scalar_agg_input`; `min`
and `max` order any non-null value (`fold_extreme`); a node, relationship or path argument is the named error
`<f>(): expected a value, got a graph element`. `count(n)` over a node is unchanged. `DISTINCT` forms, grouping keys,
`WITH` pipelines and the single-aggregate fast path all share `update_acc`, so all are covered.

Not changed: an unaliased column keeps the text-derived upper-case name (`MAX(x)`, `COUNT(x)`, `SUM(x)`), which
the TCK's Aggregation2 expects in lower case, so those scenarios still fail on the column name; an aggregate argument
is still a variable or a property, so `max(x + 1)`, `max(1)` and `max($p)` are parse errors, not wrong results
(row 159). The integer type of `sum` is row 158.

## 158. `sum` of integers returned a float — CLOSED

Found in the QL-AG review (2026-10-23). `UNWIND [1,2,3] AS x RETURN sum(x)` and `MATCH (n:N) RETURN SUM(n.v)` over
integer properties returned `6.0` / `30.0`. openCypher returns an integer for all-integer input, a float if any input is a
float, and null for no input.

Confirmed on v0.9.2 by reading `git show v0.9.2:crates/core-query/src/cypher/exec.rs`: `AggAcc::Sum { val: f64, has_value }`
finishes as `Value::Float(val)`; the tag was not built. The existing unit tests in `exec.rs` pinned `f(30.0)`.

**CLOSED by QL-AG (0.10).** `AggAcc::Sum` keeps an exact `i64` total and a float total; integers add with `checked_add`
(an overflow is the error `sum(): integer overflow`), and the result is an integer unless a float was seen. `avg` stays a
float, as openCypher has it, and so do `stDev`, `stDevP` and the percentiles (`percentileDisc` returns the input value).
This changes the output type of `sum` over integers, so it is a `### Changed` changelog entry.

## 159. Aggregate arguments that are expressions, literals or parameters are refused — DEFERRED to 0.11

`max(x + 1)`, `max(1)` and `max($p)` are parse errors: `AggArg` is `Star`, `Var`, `Prop` or `Distinct` of those. It is a
refusal, not a wrong result. Not a small grammar change: every site that reads an `AggArg` (`distinct_value`,
`stat_input`, `scalar_agg_input`, `update_acc`, variable interning, the unaliased column name) would need an expression
evaluator over the row, and the unaliased column name for an expression is a released-format question. Deferred to 0.11.

## 160. Booleans did not compare: `false < true` was null — CLOSED

Found by QL-10 (the openCypher TCK, Quantifier7 [3]). Present in released 0.9.x (checked against v0.9.2,
whose `filter::is_orderable_pair` admits numbers and strings only, and whose `min` / `max` accumulators
read `numeric_val`).

- `RETURN false < true`, `false <= true`, `true > false` and `true >= true` gave null.
- In a `WHERE` the null dropped the row: `MATCH (n) WHERE n.flag > false RETURN n` returned nothing on
  0.9.2 and on the 0.10 branch before QL-VE, whatever the data.
- `min(n.flag)` and `max(n.flag)` over a boolean property gave null.
- `ORDER BY n.flag` was right (false first): it sorts by `cmp_values`, which orders booleans.

**Severity: Medium**: a silently empty or null answer for a well-formed query.

**Fix (QL-VE):** `is_orderable_pair` orders two booleans (false before true) for both `eval_cmp` and
`eval_cmp3`; the range index's `lookup_range` orders a `Bool` bound in the same way (`OrderClass::Bool`),
so the index and the scan keep one answer (the query layer still hands a boolean bound to the scan); `min`
and `max` order booleans through QL-AG's `fold_extreme`, which the bundle-19 merge kept in place of QL-VE's
property-only change, so a bare variable (`UNWIND [true, false] AS b`) is covered too. A boolean and a value of another type still do
not order (`true < 1` is null). Evidence: `row_160_booleans_order_false_before_true` in
`crates/core-query/src/cypher/tests/ql_ve.rs` (`<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`, `WHERE`, `ORDER BY`, `min`, `max`),
the `index_matches_scan` differential, and the TCK's Quantifier7 [3], whose five scenarios now pass.

## 161. `statement()` and `transaction()` read the clock on every call — CLOSED in 0.10 (QL-CL)

Allocated by the controller for bundle-19. Introduced in the 0.10 cycle with QL-TF; never released (0.9.x has no
namespaced temporal functions).

- `date.statement()`, `date.transaction()` and the same on `localdatetime` and `datetime` call `temporal::clock`,
  which calls `now()` each time. openCypher holds one instant for every `statement()` call in a statement and one
  for every `transaction()` call in a transaction.
- Two calls in one statement can therefore return different values, and so can `statement()` and
  `transaction()` in the same statement. Two TCK Temporal10 [12] scenarios depend on this and pass or fail with
  clock timing, so the TCK baselines are set at the stable floor.
- `realtime()` reading the clock on every call is what openCypher specifies.

**Severity: Low**: the values differ by the time between two calls, not by a date or offset.

**CLOSED in 0.10 (QL-CL).** The clock is read once per statement and once at transaction BEGIN, and the instants are held for
the length of the statement or transaction: `date()`, `datetime()`, `localdatetime()` and the `.statement()` forms return the
statement's instant (in the function's type and offset), `.transaction()` the transaction's, and `.realtime()` still reads the
clock. The statement's instant is read when a depth-counted guard is first taken, at the very top of every statement entry
point (`GraphDb::query`, `query_masked`, `query_write`, `query_write_authz`, the snapshot's `query` and `query_masked`,
`Transaction::execute`), before the statement is lexed, because a constant property map folds at parse time; `core-query`'s
`execute` and `execute_planned_union` and `exec_write_stmt` join it. A subscription's query is a statement of its own
(`StatementClock::fresh`) on both the direct and the group-commit delivery paths. The transaction's instant is recorded
at BEGIN by `Transaction::new` and by each role-scoped transaction body (HTTP, Bolt, Python) and set around each
statement. The instants live in thread-local state in `crates/core-query/src/cypher/temporal.rs`, not in the evaluator's
context, because the evaluator is called from
many sites that share no context. Outside a guard (a library caller of the evaluator) each call still reads the clock.
Evidence: `crates/core-query/src/cypher/tests/ql_cl.rs` (8 tests, a counter clock, no sleeps),
scoped-transaction tests in `crates/server/src/tx.rs`, `crates/bolt/src/messages/tx.rs` and
`bindings/python/tests/test_transactions.py`, and `crates/core-api/tests/ql_cl.rs` (a literal `CREATE` property map,
`UNWIND … SET` over the per-row overlay, `SET` over 50 rows, `UNWIND … CREATE`, `SET n += {…}`, `MERGE … ON CREATE SET`, a
two-statement transaction and its writes: one value each); the TCK's Temporal10 [12] scenarios are stable.

Commits: `52819fb7..ea5dd8f0` (`7c6dfbed`, `4d35cc30`, `ea5dd8f0`), merged in bundle-20. On the bundle, three
release-build and three debug-build TCK runs each gave 2416 passed (0.7268, strict 0.7208), and the function
table no longer leaves `Temporal10.feature` out of its counts.

## 162. A reader's derived edges and weights against the live handle — CLOSED, not a defect on 0.10

Reported on 2026-10-22 by the v0.9.4 review as a side observation of its row 154 probe: `db.reader()`
disagreed with the live handle on rule-derived edges. 2559 of 5345 reader checks had null weights
(`r.wa`, `r.wb`, `r.wc`) where the handle had values, and 487 were missing derived edges (seed 1, step
14: six `SAME` edges at `p4`). That probe was not kept.

Investigated by RD-1 on `release/0.10` at `af45ce12` with a new seeded probe. It runs 12 seeds of 60
steps over six nodes and three weighted rules (`region`, `team` and `tier`, weights `wa`, `wb` and
`wc`), all deriving the one edge type `SAME`, so the rules share node pairs. Each step is a
source `SET` that asserts or retracts edges, several `SET`s in one commit (a transaction on
`SharedDb`, one statement on `GraphDb`), a rule creation, a rule deletion, a snapshot, a reopen, or
64 unrelated commits to cross a fold. After every step it compares the live handle with a fresh
`GraphDb::reader()`, and `SharedDb::read()` with `SharedDb::reader()` (the published snapshot). It
compares every edge with its properties, `node_edges` for every node with its `derived` flag, and
the counts.

- **0.10 (`af45ce12`):** 0 differences in 720 checks for each reader. Those checks covered 9704
  edge rows, every one carrying a rule weight, and 19408 `derived: true` flags.
- **v0.9.3 (scratch build of the tag, the same probe with `r.wa`, `r.wb` and `r.wc` columns in place
  of `properties(r)`, and no transactions or reader counts, which 0.9.3 does not have):** 388 of 720
  checks differ on edges or weights, for both readers alike. 277 of those have null weights only,
  and 111 are also missing derived edges; no check had an extra edge. 602 of 720 differ on
  `node_edges`, after folds, snapshots and reopens too. The reader never reported `derived: true` (982 of those mismatches differ on the
  flag alone, and 222 on the edge set too). The edge and weight differences clear at the next fold.
  The flag difference does not.

The three 0.9.x symptoms are already ledger rows, closed in 0.10:

- **Null weights** are row 124. `CommitDelta` carried no edge properties, and a rule writes
  `weight_prop` with no WAL record. 0.10 carries the commit's edge-property entries in the delta
  (`c184a547`).
- **Missing derived edges** are row 117. The delta's inserts were replayed before its retractions,
  so a commit that retracts and re-derives an edge dropped it from the tail. The probe's misses
  are only missing edges, gone at the next fold, which fits that mechanism. 0.10 nets each triple to its final state
  (`6005b407`).
- **`derived: false`** is row 114 (`6005b407`, `80f1f0bc`).

No v0.9.x tag contains any of these commits, and the v0.9.4 branch has the same
`crates/core-api/src` and `crates/core-rules/src` as v0.9.3. So the behaviour shipped in every 0.9
release, through every reader path: `SharedDb::reader()`, `ReaderSnapshot::query` and the role-token
HTTP read routes. No engine code changed for this row. The probe is kept as a regression test with 4
fixed seeds of 60 steps: `row_162_a_reader_sees_the_live_handles_derived_edges_and_weights`
(`crates/core-api/tests/reader_live_parity.rs`). It asserts zero differences for both readers.
